847. MARY RIORDEN was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of February, 1 shawl, value 9s.; 2 shirts, value 7s.; 1 shift, value 4s.; 1 pair of stays, value 8s.; 1 pair of shoes, value 8s.; 2 gowns, value 13s.; 1 gown piece, value 7s.; 1 bonnet, value 4s.; 2 caps, value 3s.; 1 collar, value 5s.; 1 coat, value 3l.; 1 pair of trousers, value 10s., and 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of John Galton.
MR. BODKIN conducted the prosecution.
JOHN GALTON. I live at Barnsbury-row, Islington. The prisoner was in my service in February last, and had been so for two months. On the 19th of February she was sent out on an errand by my wife - she was brought backby two watchmen in a quarter of an hour, with some articles of clothing, the whole of which belong to us; she was crying, and I thought some accident had happened - she said nothing relating to these things to me.
JAMES ESSEX. I am a watchman of Islington. I met the prisoner at half-past six that evening, with a bundle over her shoulder; I asked what she had got; she said clothing; I said I had some suspicion it was not right, that she must go to the watch-house, and if it was right she should be let go - in going along she said, "Let me go home;" I said, "You shall if it is not too far;" she said, "I live up here," and took me to her master's, who owned the property; while we were examining the bundle she got out backwards - another watchman pursued and took her.
AUGUSTUS FREEMAN. I am a watchman. I was on duty - when we came up to the prisoner she said she had some blankets going to be washed, I looked and saw it was wearing-apparel, and we took her to her master's - she made her way to the kitchen, and got out the back way; I missed her crying, and took her about two hundred yards from the house.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 18. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-181]
Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
848. WILLIAM PIPER, WILLIAM WALLIS and THOMAS WALLIS, were indicted for feloniously breaking and entering a building, within the curtilage of the dwelling-house of Edwin Hill and others, and stealing 1 saw, value 1s.; 2 gimblets, value 2d., and 1 hammer, value 6d., the goods of the said Edwin Hill and others; 1 pair of shoes, value 3s., and one round frock, value 1s., the goods of George Cox; 1 waistcoat, value 2s.; 1 razor, value 9d., and 4 ounces weight of tobacco, value 9d., the goods of Thomas Walker .
MR. BODKIN conducted the prosecution.
EDWIN HILL. I live at Bruce Castle, Tottenham. I have more than one partner; Cox and Walker were employed in our garden - there is a garden-house, used for keeping tools, within the ring-fence of our premises; it is about one hundred yards from our house, but within the wall.
GEORGE COX. I was in the employ of Messrs. Hill and others at Tottenham; I worked there three years, and know the garden-house very well - I remember finding it open one morning, but I cannot tell the day; I had fastened it the night before, as I did every night; it was broken open - when I fastened it at six o'clock in the evening, there was a saw, two gimblets, and a hammer, belonging to Mr. Hill, a round frock and a pair of shoes belonging to me, and a waistcoat belonging to Walker - at a quarter-past six o'clock in the morning I found it open, and these things were gone; the window was shut and fastened the night before, but there was a hole - the glass was broken; I do not know the prisoner.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Are you paid for your work there? A. Yes - I am a pauper; the officers pay me - I am not paid by Mr. Hill; I believe this was after Christmas; I think I can swear it was - I had not seen the prisoners there; I did not see this property again till last week.
THOMAS WALKER. I was employed with Cox at this place; I went to the shed between six and seven o'clock in the morning, just after him - there was snow on the ground, and I saw the foot marks of two men; the heel of one looked a good deal larger than the other; I traced them to the bottom of the garden, and then they got over the wall - I did not try to trace them any farther.
Cross-examined. Q. Where were the marks? A. Down one side; we traced them about twenty rods from the window - the snow was all over the place; Cox locked up the garden-house in my presence - we took the key to Mr. Hill, and we did so every night; I am sure the marks were of men's feet.
MR. BODKIN. Q. Were the foot marks close up to the building? A. Yes.
JOHN FOWLER. I am constable of Tottenham. On the 27th of February I searched Piper's house, behind the Roebuck, at Tottenham - I found this saw and two gimblets there; he was then in custody.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you see his wife? A. Yes, she did not show them to me - I took several duplicates from her, but did not take her into custody.
MR. BODKIN. Q. Did you take any notice of the feet of either of them? A. Thomas Wallis wears a very thicksoled shoe, having had his thigh broken - I have seen him wear such a shoe; the sole is high as well as the heel - it would make the same impression as a thin one.
JAMES GRIFFITHS. I am constable of Tottenham. I searched the house of the Wallis' - they live together at Tottenham; I found this waistcoat there - Thomas Wallis had left before I went; I took him near Buntingford, in Hertfordshire, on the 4th of March - in coming home he said he was very sorry that he ran away, but he was in hope he should not be taken; I found him in bed - he called me up and said he knew my voice, and knew what I wanted him for; I have not noticed his foot.
MR. HILL re-examined. Q. Did you ever notice the elder Wallis' feet? A. Yes, he was digging on my ground about a year ago, and he wore a thin shoe to make equality - that would make a different impression on snow; I know this saw to be mine - I do not know the gimblets.
Cross-examined. Q. Do you know when they were last safe? A. No; I cannot say whether I have lost saws before, it is very likely I may.
GEORGE COX. I know this saw well and this small gimblet - they were in the shed.
Cross-examined. Q. How many tools were there? A. I suppose half a dozen spades, shovels, and pick-axes.
THOMAS WALKER. This is my waistcoat, and was in the shed on the night in question - here is where I had cut three buttons off it.
JURY. Q. How long had you had it? A. Three-quarters of a year - it is such as the parish officers furnish to paupers; it is darned in the sleeve.
William Wallis 's Defence. My mother can swear she darned it herself.
ESTHER WALLIS. I am the daughter of Thomas Wallis; I know this waistcoat; my mother bought it twelve months ago last Friday, in Petticoat-lane, for 2s. 6d. - I know it again; I remember the officer taking it away.
COURT. Q. Do you remember the day of the month? A. Yes, it was twelve months ago last Friday - it was too small for my father, and my mother darned it; I do notknow that it was worn out at one of the pockets - I know Petticoat-lane very well; I then lived with a person named Thorn, in the Dog-row, a broom-maker - I took a walk with my mother to buy it; I do not know whether this is the colour they wear at Tottenham.
ELEANOR LAWRENCE. My husband is a carpenter, and lives at No. 9, Queen-street. This saw was broken by William Piper, at St. Katharine's Docks, and he had to pay for it; it belonged to a carpenter, who came for 2s. 6d. for it, and I saw Piper's wife pay it - I was sitting in her house at the time; this was three years ago, when the Docks began.
MR. BODKIN. Q. What is your husband? A. A carpenter, and he works for Mr. Slater, in Hackney-road - he was discharged some time ago when they were slack, but he is gone back again; I am sister-in-law to Piper - I did not look at the saw myself, but I heard them say there was the name of Hill on it. I do not know Mr. Hill, of Tottenham.
COURT. Q. Were you before the Magistrate? A. No; I did not hear that an out-house had been broken open; I heard my brother was taken up - I now live at No. 9, Queen-street, Clerkenwell; it was three years ago he broke this saw.
THOMAS WALKER re-examined. Q. Did you have the accident to break this saw? A. Yes, sawing an appletree two years ago, and I kept it back some time that Mr. Hill might not see it - it is called a lock-saw.
T. WALLIS - GUILTY. Aged 50.
PIPER - GUILTY. Aged 21.
Transported for Life.
W. WALLIS - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300415-182]
849. WILLIAM WALLIS and WILLIAM PIPER were again indicted for that they, on the 26th of February, at Tottenham, the shop of Thomas Newsom, there situate, feloniously did break and enter, and steal therein 3 bushels of apples, value 15s.; 40 oranges, value 3s.; 3 pecks of onions, value 5s.; 25lbs. weight of pork, value 20s.; 1 coat, value 10s.; 1 jacket, value 20s.; 1 pair of shoes, value 10s.; 10 knives, value 2s.; 4 forks, value 1s.; 1 bag, value 1s.; 1 pair of mittens, value 6d.; 2 razors, value 2s.; 13 boot-laces, value 1s.; 1 bed-wrench, value 1s.; 1 sack, value 1s.; 8s. in copper monies, and 1 piece of foreign coin, value 1d., the property of the said Thomas Newson ; and THOMAS WALLIS and JANE WALLIS were indicted for feloniously receiving the same, well knowing them to have been stolen.
MR. BODKIN conducted the prosecution.
THOMAS NEWSOM. I am a gardener, and live at Tottenham - I had a shop there in February last, but neither I nor any of my family slept there. On the night of the 26th of February, between eleven and twelve o'clock, I left the articles stated all safe in the shop; there is a door leads from the shop to the stairs of the dwelling-house - I fastened the shop; I went next morning between five and six o'clock - I unlocked the door of the shop, but could not get in; a man going by with a light came and assisted me: we pushed the door open and found the obstruction was occasioned by a sack being hung over the fan-light over the door - I missed all the articles stated, and the copper money and foreign coin from a desk; I went to the back door and found it on the latch - I had bolted it the night before; I looked up and saw a back room window open, and marks on the tiles as if men had got in there; I am not sure whether it had been shut the night before - the door leading to the stairs was open; which I had shut and latched - I saw several footsteps, and traced one across the gate-way, and across the field to a stile leading to Scotland-green, and on to the pavement, where I lost them; this was between six and seven o'clock; there were two or three persons footsteps at the back of my house, but it was only one I traced so far.
WILLIAM TALBOT. I am a farmer, and live at Tottenham. On the morning of the 27th of February I was out early in my field, which is between the prosecutor's and the house in which Wallis lives - I went up a private lane, and found the fence broken down; I then found some person had been crossing there - as I was returning I saw Griffiths and the other constable tracing some footsteps across my field; I came down to where the fence was broken, and found some footsteps, which I traced over another fence into Mr. Newsom's garden; I saw Thomas Wallis, who was between Mr. Newsom's and his own house - he appeared to be raking.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Did you see the rake? A. I cannot say I did; I did not speak to him - I was thirty or forty yards from him; I did not ask what business he had there - this was about seven o'clock; I told the officer.
JAMES GRIFFITHS. I am constable of Tottenham. On the morning after this robbery, I went with Fowler to the prosecutor's about seven o'clock - I found footsteps of two persons as it appeared to me; they went from there to within thirty or forty yards of Wallis' house - I got a warrant and went to Wallis'; I saw Martha Wallis first, and then Jane Wallis, the mother - I said I had a warrant to search for stolen property; she said "For God's sake, Mr. Griffiths, do'nt make any uproar in the parish - I will let you know where they all are;" the daughter then said to her, "I will tell you what mother, the first time I knew my brother kept company with Bill Piper, I knew there never would be any good come of it" - the mother then went up stairs; I and the daughter followed; I saw something which appeared to be a bed, but it was not - she took off the top clothes, and under them was the whole of the property; the prosecutor was with me and claimed it - I took William Wallis and Piper the same day; I found on Wallis, 2s. 1 1/2d. in copper, and among it this coin: there was only a knife on Piper - I took them past the prosecutor's house- Mrs. Newsom stood at the door, and said, "You good for nothing rogues, how could you rob such poor people as us?" Piper said, "D - n your eyes, why did not you take your pig home with you;" I knew old Wallis had left home, and I took him in Herts - I know Mr. Robinson's hand-writing; this examination is signed by him.
Cross-examined. Q. Is Mrs. Newsom here? A. No. I went over the ground and saw the marks of two persons who had gone in different tracks the same way; I heard Mr. Newsom state that the marks were traced to within one hundred yards of Wallis, but I traced them in a different way and nearer the house - Jane Wallis told me she would give up all, and if she had known they had been Mr. Newsom's she would have taken them back.
JOHN FOWLER. I am a constable. I searched Piper'shouse, and found a pair of gaiters and stockings very wet, as if they had been lately used, and this chisel, which I applied to the marks on the desk at the prosecutor's, and they corresponded exactly.
THOMAS NEWSOM. This coin was in the desk at my house, and the property found at Wallis' was mine.
Cross-examined. Q. Where was this coin? A. By itself in the desk, where the copper monies were; I saw it safe the night before.
The examinations were here put in and read.
William Piper, being asked by me if he had any thing to say, cautioning him, that what he said would be put down in writing, and used in evidence; neither promise nor threat was made or held out to him, but he voluntarily saith, "I went with Wallis last night to Islington, and we returned together about twelve o'clock at night; we went to my father-in-law's, in Benson's-yard, Tottenham - we staid there about ten minutes; we parted at my father-in-law's - I went directly home to my lodgings, and went to bed about one o'clock; I know nothing about the articles now produced." The mark of X WM. PIPER.
(Signed) W. ROBINSON.
William Wallis, the son, being asked by me if he had any thing to say, cautioning him, that what he said would be put down in writing, and used in evidence (neither threat nor promise was made or held out to him), but he voluntarily says -"Yesterday evening, after work, I went to Islington with William Piper; we returned to Tottenham about half-past twelve o'clock at night - we went together to Piper's mother-in-law's house, in Benson's-yard; Piper went home to his lodgings, and I went home to my father - about a quarter to one o'clock I had occasion to go into the yard; I found all the articles now produced, tied up in two sacks behind the privy - I took them into the house, and put them in the room below stairs; I opened the sacks, and I saw in one of them a dead pig, "which I took out and laid it on the table - I then went to bed; in the morning, when I got up, I told my father what I had found behind the privy." The mark X of W. WALLIS.
Before me, W. ROBINSON, 27th February, 1830.
William Wallis ' Defence. My father knew nothing of the robbery, and my mother was asleep.
Piper's Defence. I left Wallis and went home; this chisel belonged to me when I worked at East Barnet.
PIPER - GUILTY. Aged 21.
W. WALLIS - GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Life.
JANE WALLIS - GUILTY. Aged 51.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
T. WALLIS - NOT GUILTY.

Before Mr. Common Sergeant.

851. JOHN RICHARDS was indicted for stealing, on the 2nd of March, 2 live tame rabbits, price 5s., the property of James Reed.
LOT FLETCHER. The prisoner brought these two rabbits to sell at my house in Slater-street, Bethnal-green, on Wednesday evening, about seven or eight o'clock; he said they were his own - I gave 2s. for them; I went to the office next day - two persons came together, but the prisoner had the money.
ELLEN REED. I am the wife of James Reed - he is at sea. I bought these two rabbits for my little boy twelve months ago - I lost them early last month; I had fed them about six or half-past six o'clock in the evening, and fastened them in the hutch - I went to fill the kettle, and when I returned I missed them; I am certain there are them, by the marks on them - the prisoner came to my premises and wished my little boy to join with him in a pigeon, the week before I lost the rabbits; I live in Chapman-street, St. George's in the East.
JAMES MORGAN. I got these rabbits at Fletcher's; one of my men told me they were there, and I took the son and this witness - they both knew them.
Prisoner's Defence. I left my mother at five o'clock on Wednesday morning, as I meant to turn steady and mind my work; this gentleman came and took me - a boy talking with me ran away, but I did not run.
GUILTY. Aged 18. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-185]
852. JOHN ADAMS and WILLIAM BARRATT were indicted for stealing, on the 3rd of April, 18 handkerchiefs, value 3l. 10s., the goods of Jane Puzey and Martha Newbery.
JANE PUZEY. I am in partnership with Martha Newbery - we keep a shop in Holborn. On the 3d of April we lost some handkerchiefs from the shop, but did not miss them till they were brought back; these are them - they have our shop mark on them; I had seen them about two hours before.
THOMAS HOBBS. I am a Police-officer. I was going up Holborn, and saw Barratt standing at the corner of a court; Adams went into the prosecutor's shop - I turned back and passed the shop on the opposite side of the way; I stood some time till Adams brought out these handkerchiefs; he put on his hat and put them in it; he walked on- I followed him, and Barratt and another followed me; I stopped looking at a picture shop till they all came together - I followed them; they saw me - all threw off their hats and ran off; the two prisoners were taken - I took up one hat, with these six handkerchiefs in it; I am quite sure Adams is the boy who went into the shop, but they had changed hats before I came up to them - Barratt was near the shop when Adams went in; and he passed the shop to see if he was coming; I kept my eyes upon them from the time they pulled off their hats and ran - I took Barratt.
Barratt. The witness said he saw me have a cap on all the while, and when a witness was speaking at the office, he said, "Hush! hush!" - Witness. He had a cap on at first, and then the one who got away had the cap, and got off with it.
JOSEPH ROBINSON. I was a Police-constable. On the 3rd of April I was passing Lincoln's Inn-fields about six o'clock in the evening - I heard Stop thief! called, and saw Barratt running across the fields; he threw off his hat, and I saw some handkerchiefs fall out of it - I caught him, and the hat was brought to me by a young man; I saw the same hat taken up, and these handkerchiefs were in it.
Barratt. Q. How long did you see me run? A. About three minutes, as near as possible; the hat he threw off was, no doubt, for fear I should see him.
THOMAS HOBBS. These six handkerchiefs were given to me in the crowd; I do not know by whom.
Adams' Defence. I was taken; I asked for my hat, and one was brought with some handkerchiefs in it - I said it did not belong to me, and mine was brought; the man who took me could prove whether I had any handkerchiefs in my hat, but they would not let him speak at the office.
THOMAS HOBBS. I went into the clerk's office at the Magistrate's, and heard Robinson tell a person he would not be wanted.
JOSEPH ROBINSON. There was a young man came to the office, but the Magistrate had heard our depositions, and did not make any inquiry about any further witness; to the best of my knowledge he was the person who took up the handkerchief - when I came out he said, "Shall I be wanted any more;" I said, "I suppose not, the Magistrate seems satisfied."
ADAMS - GUILTY. Aged 16.
Confined Three Months.
BARRATT - GUILTY. Aged 16.
Confined Four Months.
[trialAccount t18300415-186]
853. MARTHA BURGESS was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of February, 20 yards of gingham, value 10s., the goods of James Veitch.
JAMES VEITCH. On the 20th of February I lost my gingham from my shop door in Ratcliff-highway; it was partly out, and could be taken without going into the shop; I did not miss it till the Policeman brought the prisoner in with it - this is it.
DAVID LOVICK. I was a Police-officer. I saw the prisoner take this from the door - it was partly out; she crossed into the middle of the road, and I took her back to the shop - it had been pinned on a rail in the shop; I saw her working at it as I passed - I returned, and saw her again; she tried four times before she got it.
Prisoner's Defence. I picked it up, and did not see where it came from - I was in great distress.
GUILTY. Aged 40. - Confined Two Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-187]
854. THOMAS READ was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of March, 1 bridle, value 5s., the goods of John Abrahams; 2 tugs, value 2s.; part of a pair of reins, value 1s., and 2 back bands, value 2s., the goods of John Cuttell .
JOHN ABRAHAMS. I am a green-grocer; I rent a stall at the Nag's Head. On the morning of the 11th of March, at a quarter past four o'clock, I went there and found the gates open; a fishmonger had gone out before I got there, and left the gates open - I saw a man with his face to the wall, on the left-hand side, about twenty yards from Mr. Cuttell's door; I knocked at his door, and told him some person was in the stable - he went and got an officer; I went back with the officer, and missed my bridle, which I had left there over night - I found it about thirty yards from the stable, laying in the yard; the prisoner was found in the yard.
JOHN CUTTELL. I am landlord of the yard. I found some harness in different parts of the yard - the prisoner was in the yard, and no one else; nothing was found on him - he was behind a rubbish-cart; five of us were nearly two hours looking for him.
Prisoner. Q. Were not your gates open at all hours of the night? A. No; there is a fisherman goes out every morning, at half-past three o'clock, and he leaves the gates open - the premises are extensive, and persons might get into the yard; there is a hole in the back of the premises, which leads to the barracks - two of these stables were locked; they got a ladder and got up in the rack.
GEORGE HORNSBY. I am an officer. I took the prisoner, and found a dessert-knife and latch-key on him - the stables were not fastened.
Prisoner's Defence. I went into the yard, and laid down in one of the carts; I knew no more till the officer came - the stable door was off the hinges.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300415-188]
855. CHARLES RUMSEY was indicted for stealing, on the 2nd of April, 3lbs. weight of cheese, value 18d., the goods of Jacob Holmes and Thomas Russell.
JACOB HOLMES. I am in partner ship with Thomas Russell - we live in Chiswell-street. On the 2nd of April we lost a piece of cheese; I did not see it taken, but a young man told me of it - I had seen it not above a minute before, on a board within the sash, which was open.
RICHARD BOHLEN. I was passing on the 2nd of April, and saw the prisoner take the cheese from the shop; he put his hand over the board - it was six or eight inches within the shop; I pursued, and took him in Type-street- there was another with him; the prisoner dropped the cheese as I collared him, and Mr. Holmes took it up.
EDWARD PIMSTONE. I am an officer, and took him - he said he stole it because he was hungry.
Prisoner's Defence. It was not me that took it.
GUILTY. Aged 19. - Transported for Seven Years.

856. HENRY READ was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of February, 1 pair of shoes, value 3s., the goods of Ralph Wilcoxon.
SAMUEL WELLS. I am foreman to Ralph Wilcoxon, a shoemaker, of Oxford-street. On the 25th of February the prisoner came and asked for a pair of shoes; he tried on several, and fixed on one pair, which were to be 7s. 6d. - he then said he was going next door, to have something to eat, and would get change; I said we could give him change - he hesitated, then felt his pocket, and said he had lost a sovereign; I said he did not look like a man who had a sovereign to lose - he said he had: I said I had my doubts, and he could have no objection to my feeling his pockets, which I did, and found these shoes, which are my master's.
PETER KAIN. I took the prisoner, and have the property.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I lost the sovereign just before I went into the shop, from a hole in my pocket.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury and Prosecutor.
Fined One Shilling and Discharged.

857. AGNES PURTLE was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of April, 6 yards of ribbon, value 4s., the goods of James Hawkins.
SUSANNAH WALTERS. I serve in the shop of Mr. James Hawkins, in Sidney's-alley - he keeps a bonnetshop; I saw this ribbon safe on Saturday night, and was told on Sunday morning it was gone - the prisoner was my servant; I searched the house, and found the ribbon under the mattress of her bed - I charged her with it; she denied it - I said it was of no use denying it, for I had found it; she then begged of me to forgive her - she had been three months with me, and had behaved very well; I had a good character with her.
ELIZA SUSANNAH OWEN. I got up that morning to take a walk, and saw the prisoner placing some amber coloured ribbon up to her cap, to see how it looked - I asked Mrs. Walters if she had given her the ribbon; she said No - the ribbon was then found under the mattress of her bed.
JOHN FIRNS. I was called in to take the prisoner - I asked her if she took the ribbon; she said Yes, out of the drawer.
One witness gave the prisoner a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury and Prosecutor.
Judgment Respited .

858. THOMAS PEARCE was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of March, 1 watch, value 15s.; 1 watchchain, value 6d., and 1 watch-key, value 6d., the goods of Arthur Edward Meggs.
THOMAS AMES. I am a constable of Shadwell. I went to the shop of Lawson, a pawnbroker, on the 17th of March; I saw the prisoner, who Mr. Lawson said had come to redeem a watch which had been stopped that morning - I asked how he came to get a watch out without a duplicate; he said a young man outside sent him in, and said he could get it - I asked him to point out the young man; he said he could not see him, and did not know his name, nor where he lived.
SAMUEL WILLIAMS. On the 16th of March I received the watch in pawn, I believe from the prisoner, but I cannot positively swear it - I live with Mr. Lawson.
EDWARD MEGGS. I am the son of Arthur Edward Meggs. This is his watch - we missed it out of the back room below stairs, on the 16th of March; the prisoner was at our house sometimes, but I cannot be positive whether he was there on the 16th of March.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Is that your watch? A. Yes, my father gave it me.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300415-192]
859. MATTHEW NOWLAND was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of February, 1 coat, value 6s., and 1 snuff-box, value 2s., the goods of William John Holt.
WILLIAM JOHN HOLT. I missed my coat and snuffbox on the 18th of February from the lobby of No. 6, Finsbury-square, where I reside as clerk - I missed a great coat in about a week, and have not found it since; but I have this snuff-box, which was in the pocket of it- I had had it so long that I knew it immediately from some chips on it; it had some snuff in it when I lost it, and some of the same snuff was in it when it was found - the prisoner used to come to the house, but I never saw him.
ESTHER ADLINGTON. I am servant to Mr. Ewington. On the 18th of February the prisoner brought some coals to the house, and on the 25th he brought two sacks more - he took some up stairs, and some down; he passed the passage or hall, where the coats hang in general.
JOHN GRAVES. I live in the same house with Medcraft in Hart-street. I received a coat from a little girl, who lives with my landlady, about the 18th of February; it was a black coat, but I never opened it - it was a close body coat; the prisoner came to my room a little before one o'clock, and asked for a black coat - I said, "There is one hangs over the chair;" he said, "I left it when you were out;" Mrs. Loveday had come in, called me Johnny, asked if it would fit me, and then I saw it was a close body coat, and too small for me - I saw that it had a bit of red tape at the top, and said "Where did it come from? it must have been hung up at some shop;" the prisoner laughed, and took it away - he gave me a bit of snuff from a box, the colour of this, and it had some drawing on it. On the 25th of February he came again in a great flurry, and asked if his boots were done; he had a great coat on then.
SARAH MEDCRAFT. The prisoner came into my passage, and went into the yard - I ran down, and he said,"Will you be so kind as to give Mr. Graves this coat," and he took it out of a coal-sack - I took it from him, hung it across a chair; and when my little girl came from school, I sent it to Mr. Graves; it was a close body coat.
MARY ANN LOVEDAY. I saw the prisoner in Mr. Graves' room, with a coat under his arm - I said, "What have you got?" he said, "A coat" - I said, "Will it fit our Johnny?" he said No; I took it, and saw a piece of red tape to hang it up by - the prisoner took it, and went away; he said, "I know where this shall fetch me eight pegs;" I saw a little yellow snuff-box, with a picture drawn on the top of it - I think it was this one.
JOHN GARDNER. The prisoner came, and asked me to lend him 11/2d. towards a pot of beer, and left this box with me; it had snuff in it - this is the box; I marked it, and then I lent him 1d. to buy some tobacco.
GEORGE GREENING. I am an officer. I took the prisoner, and asked what he had done with the coat - he at first said he should tell no one, and then said he had had no coat at all.
JOHN HOLT. Mine was a black coat, and had a piece of tape to hang it on a hook - I used it in the office.
Prisoner's Defence. I know nothing of the coat - when I went to Mr. Ewington's house the servant always asked me in; I told Gardner I bought the box for 6d. of a man I did not know.
GUILTY. Aged 21. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-193]
860. WILLIAM SHAW was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of April, 1 silver snuff-box, value 5l., the goods of Edward Crockford, from his person.
EDWARD CROCKFORD. I am an estate agent. On the 13th of April I was getting into a cab - the prisoner pushed by me, and I struck at him; I put my hand to my coat pocket, and found I had lost my snuff-box - I saw him going towards Sackville-street, and then he began to run - I ran, caught him, and found the box in his hand; he had taken it from his pocket.
Prisoner. Q. Did you feel me take it out of your pocket? A. Yes, that I did.
JOSHUA FREDERICK CLEMENTS. I took the prisoner. This is the box.
Prisoner's Defence (written). As I was coming down Bond-street, I observed two men following a gentleman; my suspicions induced me to watch them - I followed them as close as I could, and saw the gentleman get into a coach; the said men were close to the carriage - I did not observe any thing wrong; turned round, walked a few paces, and then run - I saw them drop a box, which I picked up; a cry of Stop thief! was raised; the men got out of sight - I was returning with the box, which I gave him, and stated the case to him, but would hear nothing.
JURY to EDWARD CROCKFORD. Q. Did he come towards you? A. No, he ran from me, and I called Stop thief! I am certain he ran against me as I was getting into the cab.
GUILTY. Aged 18. - Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18300415-194]
861. JAMES MILTON was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of February, 1 bushel of beans, value 4s., the goods of Edward Kirby.
EDWARD KIRBY. I am a farmer at Ryeslip, beyond Harrow. On the 25th of February, at nine o'clock at night, I saw the prisoner take these beans from my barn- I was in a place adjoining to watch; he was in my service - he came into the barn, took some beans from the heap, and put them into a bag; he then went to the lower part of the yard with the bag - I waited till he came back and said, "Milton, you have stolen some beans;" he said he had not - I said, "I saw you take them, and you shall go with me;" he said, "I will go and fetch them back;" but I said, "No, that won't do, you have taken so many;" he said it was the first time - I went with him across the turnpike road, and found them under a bush.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Had he any horses to feed? A. Yes; he had been with me about a week- my horses had split beans, but these were not split; they were to be split in the barn.
EDWARD SCEENEY. I am an officer, and took the prisoner - he is married, and lives in the parish.
GUILTY. Aged 31. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18300415-195]
862. JOHN KEEGAN was indicted for stealing, on the 3rd of April, 2 shoes, value 6s., the goods of James Stack.
JAMES STACK. I am a shoemaker, and live in Holywell-street, Strand. About one o'clock on the 3rd of April, the prisoner came and said, "I want a pair shoes;" he took a pair off the hooks, and said, "These are about my size, but I can't have them now, I am going to Charing-cross;" I turned my back, and he walked out of the shop - I missed these shoes, and pursued him; he had got half way down the street - I went and said, "You scoundrel, you have a pair of shoes of mine;" I brought him back; he threw down the shoes, gave me a blow, knocked me down, and ran off - I pursued; a Thames Police-officer was coming up, and stopped him - he gave him a blow, but the officer got up and took him; he was quite sober.
Prisoner. Q. Did not I give you the shoes back again? A. When I brought you back, you threw them down and struck me over the head - you said you were in the army, and was going to Cox and Greenwood's.
JOHN GASKIN. I am a constable. I saw the prisoner running down Arundel-street; I caught him - he hit me in the eye, and made it bleed; I lost my hold of him, but I got him again and kept him.
Prisoner. I was twenty-five years in the army, and a serjeant nineteen; I have been handsomely remunerated; it is not likely I should compromise a 35l. a year salary to get 6s.
GUILTY. Aged 41. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-196]

RACHAEL BOULTON. Prisoner. I leave it to the mercy of the Court.

864. MARY JOHNSON was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of March, 13 yards of ribbon, value 10s., the goods of Robert Pite.
JOHN NOBBS. I am shopman to Mr. Robert Pite. The prisoner came in as a customer, and was there for twenty minutes, but bought nothing; the ribbons were in a drawer on the counter - I saw Mr. Pite take this ribbon from the prisoner.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. What is the reason Mr. Pite is not here? A. I do not know - he was not before the Magistrate; I do not suppose there were three words spoken which I did not hear - Mr. Pite did not say he had hung a man once for robbing him, and he would hang this girl if he could; I observed the prisoner appeared silly - I have not heard that she is so.
JOHN CLARK. I took up the prisoner - I found nothing on her.
Prisoner's Defence. I went to ask for some ribbon - he gave it me, and the man came and shoved this under my arm; the officer took it from me - I laid 1s. on the counter.
Two witnesses gave the prisoner a good character.

865. WILLIAM HOGAN was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of February, 23lbs. of pork, value 11s., the goods of John Morgan.
JOHN MORGAN. I keep a shop and sell pork, at Bromley, St. Leonard's. I lost four pieces of pork on the 24th of February; I did not see it taken, but it was brought back and I knew it was mine - it had been on my counter; the door must have been open for a person to go in - the window does not open.
JOSEPH REECE. I overtook the prisoner with the pork about two hundred yards from the shop; he was carrying it in his smock frock - he said he had bought it at Whitechapel.
HANNAH HOLT. I saw the prisoner go out of the shop backwards - I suspected he had done something wrong, and I said to my uncle he has taken something; he was brought back afterwards.
THOMAS BURFORD. I took the prisoner, and found this stick with a hook at the end of it, and the pork was lacerated with the hook.
Prisoner's Defence. I met a man who asked me to have a bit of meat which he showed me - I said I had not money for it; he wanted 7s. - I was going away, but was called back and had it for 6s.

866. WILLIAM HENDERSON was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of April, 3 loaves of bread, value 2s., the goods of Alexander Robertson.
JOHN COCKRANE. I am a baker in the employ of Mr. Alexander Robertson, of Oxford-street, On the 8th of April I lost three loaves from my basket at the corner of Wild-street, while I was round the corner serving a customer - I had not been away ten minutes; I saw the bread at the office.
JOHN McGREGOR. I was in Harley-street on the 8th of April, and saw the prisoner with two 4lb. loaves and one 2lb. loaf - I followed him into the New-road and stopped him; I asked where he bought it - he said he did not buy it at all; I asked who gave it him - he said, "No one, I took it out of a baker's basket, take the bread and let me go;" I said, "You shall go back with me, you have been long enough at this business;" I took him back and found the witness.
JOSEPH CLARK. I took the prisoner - he said he had not bought it, nor had it given him.
Prisoner's Defence. I have been out of work and have a large family.

867. GEORGE HALL was indicted for stealing, on the 3rd of April, 1 looking-glass, value 2l. 10s., the goods of Thomas Frevillion.
THOMAS FREVILLION. I keep the Rum Puncheon in Old-street . On the 3rd of April, about one o'clock, a person came in and had a glass of ale and a biscuit; he went out, and bade me good morning - I went into the parlour to get the glass, and missed a looking-glass, which had been fastened over the mantel-piece, and the window was open; I went after the man who had just left the house, as I thought he had handed it to some other person; it must have been taken out of the window- I found the prisoner in Bath-street with the glass, within five minutes after the other man had left my house- he ran away, but I never lost sight of him.
JOSEPH NEWMAN. I am a bricklayer. I heard the cry of Stop thief! and saw the prosecutor pursuing the prisoner - I took him; the prosecutor had taken the glass from him, put it down, and pursued him.
JOHN GLIBBERY. I took the prisoner - he said distress drove him to it, and he wished to be transported.
COURT to THOMAS FREVILLION. Q. When you took the glass from him, did you lose sight of him? A. No, not at all - there was no other way it could go but out of the window.
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that he met a gentleman, who employed him to go to the prosecutor's house, and that the gentleman went in and gave him the glass out at the window, to take to the Eagle-tavern, City-road - he had told the officer he wished to be transported, because he was in distressed circumstances.
GUILTY. Aged 30. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-201]
868. WILLIAM GRIFFIN was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of February, 1 handkerchief, value 3d., the goods of Daniel Larter; 4 gowns, value 40s.; 1 pelisse, value 20s.; 1 shawl, value 5s., and 2 handkerchiefs, value 18d., the goods of Sarah Bates .
DANIEL LARTER. I keep the Sun public-house in Shoreditch . On the 6th of February I lost a handkerchief - the other property is not mine, but I heard it was taken half an hour after the prisoner left the house; he had slept there on the night of the 5th.
SARAH BATES. I was in the service of Mr. Larter. The prisoner slept at the house that night - I did not miss any property of mine till Sunday at dinner time; I have never seen it since - I had seen it on the middle of Friday, and on Saturday he left us.
Prisoner. Q. Did you purchase the handkerchief yourself? A. Yes, for Mr. Larter.
THOMAS HOWLER. I received the prisoner, and he gave me this handkerchief.
MR. LARTER. This handkerchief was taken off the prisoner's neck in my parlour - I took him myself; I have every reason to believe this is my handkerchief - I have worn it, but there is no mark on it; it has not been washed.
MARY JEWSON. I work at the prosecutor's. I know this is Mr. Larter's - I can swear to my own work on it; here is one corner which I did in haste, and did badly -I did it nine months ago; it was a half handkerchief when I hemmed it.
Prisoner's Defence. I stopped there on the night in question, and left in presence of those belonging to the house - I went to Deptford, and was on board a ship some days; the prosecutor then took me, and charged me with this offence - the witness swore to the handkerchief; her conduct was marked with an infamy which induced the officer to call her to order - I bought the handkerchief on the western ocean, and wore it round my neck.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300415-202]
869. THOMAS GORLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 2nd of April, 5 silver spoons, value 2l., the goods of the Rev. Thomas Taylor.
THOMAS RANCE. I am servant to Mr. Martin Ware, of Bloomsbury-square. On the afternoon of the 2nd of April, I was out with my master's carriage in the King's-road - I went to knock at a door, and saw the prisoner in an area; our coachman saw him, and said, "What are you at there?" he said, "It is only my cap;" two more boys were standing there - the prisoner had got the window up; he came over the rails - the coachman said to me,"You go after him;" which I did, and a porter stopped him - I took off his cap, and found five spoons in it.
ROBERT KELLEY. I am a batter. I saw Rance running after the prisoner, and crying Stop thief! the prisoner was very much out of breath; I met him - he began to cry, and desired me not to stop him; I seized him by the tail of his coat - he struggled a little, and tried to get away, but I saw these spoons, and gave him to an officer.
THOMAS MARTIN. I am servant to the Rev. Thomas Taylor; these spoons were his, and were kept in the front pantry - two of them have a crest on them, and the others his initials.
WILLIAM BURTON. I took the prisoner.
Prisoner's Defence. A boy threw my cap over - he told me to go and take the spoons, and I did.
GUILTY. Aged 14. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-203]
870. SARAH ENGLISH was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of February, 3 caps, value 2s., and 2 pillowcases, value 1s., the goods of Mary Ann Gough; and 1 cap, value 5s., the goods of Elizabeth Barrel .
MARY ANN GOUGH. I am single. I lost my caps and pillow-cases on the 13th of February - they were found in the prisoner's possession; this cap is Elizabeth Barrel 's.
ELIZABETH BARREL. This cap is mine - I lodged with Gough.
THOMAS GOOK. I took the prisoner, and got these things from her box, at a house of ill-fame, where she went as a servant.
Prisoner's Defence. The caps were put into my box -I did not know they were there; I went to take them back to Miss Gough, could not find her, and left 1s. for them - I did not see her till the officer took me.
MARY ANN GOUGH. She owed me 2s., and brought me 1s.
THOMAS GOOK. The caps were found dirty - they were clean when they were taken away.
GUILTY. Aged 18. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-204]
871. CHARLES EDWARD EVANS was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of March, 1 umbrella, value 3s., and 1 stick, value 6d., the goods of Christian Bergstrom.
CHRISTIAN BERGSTROM. On the 15th of March the prisoner came to my shop in Tottenham-court-road, under pretence of buying an umbrella; he went out of the shop- I followed him, and found this umbrella and stick on him; he would not give them up - a gentleman went for an officer; he was but a few yards from the shop when I took him.
MATTHEW LOVEDAY. I am an officer, and took him; he made a great resistance - I was obliged to throw him on the ground, and take him by force; I found some copper money on him.
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that the prosecutor had given the things into his hand, and that the officer had ill-treated him.
CHRISTIAN BERGSTROM. I did not sell them, or give them to him; he did not say he wanted to take them any where.
Prisoner. I asked the price, and he said 5s.; I disputed it, and he said I might have it for 4s. - I said if he would come himself, or send his shopman, to King's Headcourt, I would give him an answer - I have been cupped and blistered at the hospital for a brain fever.
GUILTY. Aged 23. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-205]
872. REBECCA CARD was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of April, 3 half-crowns, and 2 shillings, the monies of Joseph Thompson, from his person.
JOSEPH THOMPSON. On the 9th of April I lost three half-crowns and two shillings; I felt it taken from me - the prisoner had hold of my arm at the time; she had asked me to take a walk with her, and I was going - I made some resistance when she took my money; part of it fell on the pavement, and a witness took the other from her hand.
JURY. Q. Were you sober? A. Not exactly, but I recollect what passed.
JOHN OWEN. I saw the prisoner and the prosecutor together in Brick-lane; he was very tipsy - the prisoner fell down on the top of him in the middle of the street; she wanted to lead him home, and he was not willing to go; she took hold of him to take him, and he fell down - I went with one of the Police-officers, and helped him up; the officer asked me to see the prosecutor home - the prisoner said he was her uncle, and then her father; that he lived in Winkfield-street, and then in Chick-street - I laid hold of his left arm, and in passing the brewhouse I saw the prisoner put her hand into his pocket, and take some money out - an officer was under the light, and he saw the transaction - the prosecutor did not charge her with taking it; one half-crown fell on the ground, and two halfcrowns she had in her hand - the officer ran over and seized her; he said "What have you got?" she gave him the 2s., said she had nothing more, and put her other hand behind her - he took hold of that hand, and took the two half-crowns from it; I picked up the other half-crown, and carried the prosecutor to the station in Church-street, Bethnal-green - he could not then keep on his legs.
ZACHARIAH BAKER. I am the officer. I took the 2s. from her hand, which I had seen her take from his righthand waistcoat pocket - I took these two half-crowns from her other hand, which she said had nothing in it.
THOMAS WELLS. I am barman to Mr. Martin, in Church-street - I gave the prosecutor change for a half-sovereign - I gave him three half-crowns, one shilling, and two sixpences; he was in liquor, but not drunk.
Prisoner's Defence. About ten o'clock, or half-past ten, the prosecutor met me and laid hold of my arm - he asked to go home with me; I said I was married and my husband was at sea - he then took me to that gentleman's house and called for a quartern of rum; he gave me one glass - he called for another, and wanted me to drink the whole of it; he came out and asked if he could get a bed, saying he had plenty of money to pay for it - he took his money out and showed it, and dropped it - I called the officer and said he had dropped some.
GUILTY. Aged 35. - Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-206]
873. ELIZABETH CORMACK was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of March, 1 basket, value 3s., the goods of William Fort.
WILLIAM FORT. I am a basket-maker, and live at Islington. I lost a basket on the 6th of March; I did not see who took it, but I had seen it a quarter of an hourbefore - I went and got it from the prisoner, fifty or sixty yards from my premises, near the Peacock.
JOSEPH GARRATTS. I saw the prisoner take the basket and followed her - another person called the prosecutor and we followed the prisoner till we met the officer; the basket had been under the window, against the cellar, for sale.
JAMES DENNIS. I am an officer. I took the prisoner with the basket - she made many excuses, and said she found it in the middle of the road.
Prisoner's Defence. I found it in the street - I am a widow, and have three children.
GUILTY. Aged 48. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18300415-207]
874. SARAH CLIFT was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of April, 4lbs. of bacon, value 2s., the goods of William Rowe.
DAVID LARKEY. I am servant to Mr. William Rowe, cheesemonger, of Chiswell-street. On the 5th of April the prisoner took this bacon from the window, which was open - I ran after her up Bunhill-row; I took the bacon and dropped it - I asked her to come back and put it in its place and beg pardon; she screwed herself out of my hands and ran up the row, but I took her.
WILLIAM BAKER ASHTON. I am a Policeman. I took the prisoner, and have the bacon.
THOMAS ANDERSON. I am a millwright. I saw the prisoner struggling with Larkey - I saw the bacon on the ground, and took it up.
GUILTY. Aged 25. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-208]
875. DAVID BASFORD was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of March, 1lb. weight of tea, value 5s., the goods of Edward Daker Harley.
JOSEPH MICHAEL ROSE. I am shopman to Mr. E. D. Harley, who lives near Blenheim-steps, Marlborough-street. The prisoner came and asked for an ounce of barley-sugar -I was busy; and as he was leaving the shop he was stopped with the tea, which I had put on the counter just before with some other parcels.
JAMES KENNERLEY. I am a music-copier. I saw the prisoner lurking about the shop - I saw him go and take the parcel off the counter; he put it between his legs and came out with it - I took him.
ANGELIOUS BETRAUN. I took the prisoner in charge.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was turning to come away, and felt myself seized by the arm - this parcel fell on the floor, and the witness took it up.
JOHN MICHAEL ROSE. He was stopped just at the doorway.
GUILTY. Aged 18. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-209]
876. JOHN BLAKEMAN was indicted for stealing, on the 23rd of March, 1 coat, value 30s., the goods of Matthew Robinson, Esq.
SECOND COUNT, charging it to belong to Robert Taylor.
ROBERT TAYLOR. I am servant to Matthew Robinson, Esq., of Dulwich. On the 23rd of March my great coat was lost - I missed it when I got up behind the carriage; I had seen it ten minutes before - it is now in Court.
GEORGE AVIS. My father is an officer. I was near Regent-street, and saw the prisoner put his foot on the step of the carriage, and take the coat; he went a little way, and tried to put it on, but dropped it - a soldier picked it up; I followed him till I saw him stopped.
THOMAS GILL. I am an officer. I took the coat - the prisoner came running down the street, and threw it down; it was taken up, and given to me.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 19. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-210]
877. ELIZABETH BAXTER was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of March, two sheets, value 3s.; 2 blankets, value 5s.; 1 pillow, value 2s., and 1 quilt, value 6d., the goods of James Blackwell.
JAMES BLACKWELL. The prisoner lodged at a house of mine, and I let this property to her as part of the furniture - she came on the 22nd of February, and went away on the 1st of March; she gave me no notice, but remained out all night - she returned on the Tuesday evening; I then went up stairs - as I was going into the room, shew as coming out with this counterpane tied up in a handkerchief; I asked if she had got any rent for me - she said she had not; I went into the room, put down the bedstead, and missed two sheets, two blankets, and a pillow; I asked what was become of them - she said if I would go with her to her mistress, at Portland-town, she would get her to be answerable for their return - I went, but she refused; I have never had them.
CHARLES COOK. I took up the prisoner - she said if the prosecutor would give her time, she would replace the goods; she owned she had taken them, and sold them at a rag-shop, but could not tell where unless she went to the place.
Prisoner's Defence. I was in distress - I meant to replace them, but had no time.
GUILTY. Aged 37. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-211]
878. JOHN CLEMENTS was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of March, 3 handkerchiefs, value 7s., the goods of John Cliff; and 1 pair of breeches, value 9s., the goods of Patrick Macnamara .
PATRICK MACNAMARA. I lodged at the same house with the prisoner - he slept in the same room with Cliff, not with me; I missed a pair of breeches and a pair of garters from a press in the room he slept in - these are my breeches.
JOHN CLIFF. I slept with the prisoner on the 5th of March; I went out in the morning, and left him in bed with another man - I returned at eight o'clock, and missed these three handkerchiefs.
CHARLES JOHN TAPPER. I am an officer. I took the prisoner, and found the duplicates on him.
RICHARD MORRIS. I am a pawnbroker. These breeches and handkerchiefs were pawned with me by a person much resembling the prisoner - I gave this duplicate for them.
GUILTY. Aged 26. - Transported for Seven Years.

Fourth Middlesex Jury.
879. MARY BAKER was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of February, 5 shirts, value 5s.; 4 handkerchiefs, value 2s.; 1 frock, value 6d., and 3 stockings, value 6d., the goods of John Kidd.
CATHERINE KIDD. I am the wife of John Kidd. On Ash-Wednesday I lost these articles from my children's bed-room - I had seen them on the Tuesday; a lad came and told me a person was up stairs - I went, and found the prisoner with them; I asked how she came by them- she said the door was open, and any one might have taken them as well as her; I had never seen her before.
WILLIAM IFGRAVE. I live in the same house. I saw the prisoner there with some things under her cloak - she was coming from the room; I asked where she got them - she said she had been to bring them down for a friend of hers to take care of, and asked if a person named Turner lived there; there was no such person there - she said she knew she was right, because she saw the corpse lying in the house; I called the prosecutrix, who said she was a very bad woman for taking her things; the prisoner said that the door was open, she came up by mistake, and was going to get a coffin of the parish, but that was for a person at another house, not at the prosecutor's - it did appear to be a mistake.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Did not she knock at the door on the first floor? A. Not to my knowledge - I met her there; she did not say she came to see Mrs. Turner, whose husband was lately dead, and she found those things about, and knocked at the door to mention it; a woman, named Turner, lived very near, whose husband lay dead; I have heard so since - I do not know whether the room on the second floor was left open.
DANIEL PAYNTER. I am an officer. I took the prisoner - she said she mistook the house, but Mrs. Turner's husband lay dead about fifty yards from there - she said her sister-in-law lodged on the first floor.
ELIZABETH WYERS. I was going to get a coffin for my husband, who was dead - I met the prisoner, who asked me to have a glass of something to drink; she asked me to take her to see my husband, which I did - it was dark, and she was rather in liquor; she said she would call again, and see if she could relieve me with something - my house is twenty yards from the prosecutor's.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300415-213]
Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
880. JAMES SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of April, 1 silk handkerchief, value 5s., the goods of William Stone, from his person.
WILLIAM STONE. I am an officer in the army. On the 17th of April I was going from Wimpole-street to Oxford-street, about half-past nine o'clock in the morning; I missed my pocket-handkerchief from my pocket when I got to Oxford-street - it was a new one; I had not seen the prisoner.
WILLIAM IRELAND. I was in Wimpole-street on duty; a gentleman's servant called to me, and said, "These two men have taken a handkerchief from a gentleman's pocket"- I went and took the prisoner, who Killick said had received it from the other; the prisoner took the handkerchief from his pocket, and attempted to throw it down an area; I caught it, and took him - this is it.
HENRY KILLICK. I saw the prisoner and another in Wimpole-street; I saw the other take the handkerchief from the prosecutor's pocket - the prisoner received it; they were both close to him.
WILLIAM STONE. This is my handkerchief; it was safe when I left my house in Wimpole-street.
Prisoner's Defence. I picked it up.
GUILTY. Aged 22. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-214]
881. ELLEN CRAWLEY and ELIZABETH TURPIN were indicted for stealing, on the 3rd of April, 1 basket, value 3d., and 90 oranges, value 18d., the goods of John Baines.
JOHN BAINES. I am a fruit-salesman, in Covent-garden-market. I lost this basket and oranges from my stand on the 3rd of April, about four o'clock in the afternoon; I had seen Turpin near the stand.
WILLIAM HERBERT. I am a fruiterer. I was near the prosecutor's stand; I saw Turpin take this basket of oranges, and give it to Crawley; I followed her round a corner, and took her with it - I sent to the prosecutor, who had them both taken.
WILLIAM MORGAN. I am constable of Covent-gardenmarket, and took the prisoners; in going along Turpin asked if I would let the young one go, as she knew nothing about it.
Turpin's Defence. I was going down the market, and met a friend; we went and had something to drink - she then met her husband, and they had some words; she said she had bought some oranges, and asked me to go and get them; she had left her boy with them - I went, but did not see the boy; I took up this basket, thinking it was hers - I said to this girl, "If you will take this up to the top shop, I will go and look for the boy;" that is all I know.
TURPIN - GUILTY. Aged 50.
Transported for Seven Years.
CRAWLEY - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300415-215]
882. JOHN BROOKS was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of February, 1 tea-spoon, value 3s.; 2 bottles, value 2s.; 1 half-pint of rum, value 10d.; 1 half-pint of gin, value 6d.; 2 half-crowns, and 3s. 1d. in copper monies, the property of Henry Oldham.
HENRY OLDHAM. I keep the Crown, in Long-acre -On the 26th of February, in the afternoon, I missed this spoon; the prisoner had been in the bar all day - I missed the money in the evening; the prisoner had slept there the night before - I missed three half-crowns from the till; I had gone out of the bar while he was there - I had not observed what silver was in my till, but I know the half-crowns were there; I missed two of them first - I then went out of the bar, and when I returned I found the till unlocked, and the other one gone; the till was locked when I went out - the spoons were taken from the bar parlour.
Cross-examined by MR. CRESWELL. Q. Was he not drinking all the night before? A. He had drank a little in the evening, some beer and some half-and-half; I did not see him drink any spirits or wine - he did not fall asleep in my bar; he came there on the evening of the 23rd - he was not very drunk; he frequented the house before I came there, which is fifteen months ago; I believe he is respectably connected; I lock my till when I go out, and take the key - I know there were half-crowns,shillings and sixpences in the till, but no crown pieces; I cannot say how many persons came in while he was there.
WILLIAM CLARK. I was in the house at a quarter after eleven o'clock; Mrs. Oldham had been saying she had lost some money and a tea-spoon - I said, "Who have you had in your bar?" she said, "No one but Mr. Brooks;" soon after Mr. Brooks came into the tap-room, and laid down - Mr. Oldham's brother-in-law was there: Mr. Oldham then came in, and said he had lost the money and the spoon; he saw the prisoner there, he rubbed his hand down him, and found these bottles and this spoon on him - I had a very severe struggle with him; he made very great resistance; there were three half-crowns, two shillings, one sixpence, 3s. in copper, and four farthings on him.
Cross-examined. Q. Had you been there the night before? A. I do not recollect; I had reason to recollect that evening, as I got a brace of very handsome black eyes; I gave him as good as he sent, and a little better: I cannot say whether he was drunk the night before.
GUILTY. Aged 27. - Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18300415-216]
883. MARY BARRETT was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of March, 1 shirt, value 1s., and 1 frock, value 6d., the goods of John Barker.
JOHN BARKER. I live in New Gravel-lane, Shadwell; I deal in earthenware and old clothes. About nine o'clock in the evening of the 16th of March, I was opposite my door, talking to a neighbour, and saw the prisoner come out of my house with these things under her arm - I went into my house to ask if she had been there; they said No - I pursued, and took her, twenty yards from my door: she was in a stooping position - I asked what she had been in my house for; she said she had not, but when she moved, these things fell from her; they are mine, and had been in my warehouse - she was quite a stranger.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 50.
[trialAccount t18300415-217]
884. SARAH BISHOP was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of March, 1 counterpane, value 2s.; 2 handkerchiefs, value 1s., and 2 caps, value 3s., the goods of Henry Hewitt.
SARAH HEWITT. I am the wife of Henry Hewitt, a shoemaker - we live in Wentworth-street. I have known the prisoner a year and a half or more; she came to lodge with me on the 29th of March, but she had no money, and I refused to let her in - she went away, but was brought in again between ten and eleven o'clock with the property, which is mine; she had been in my house about a quarter of an hour.
MOSES JACOBS. I live in Wentworth-street. On the 29th of March the prisoner came between ten and eleven o'clock, and wanted me to buy these articles; I said I would not have them - she said she wanted 1s. for them; I said I would not buy them if she wanted but 1d.; she then said she had brought nothing in, but wanted a shift - I called the officer and gave charge of her.
GEORGE CRAWLEY. I was on duty and took the prisoner; I heard the witness say they did not suit him - he would not have them.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. They were given me by a woman who lodged in the house with me; this wicked woman has been swearing falsely against me - the officer knows I was in such a state I did not know what I was doing; she is the ruin of hundreds - her door is always opened at seven o'clock in the evening, for poor creatures to be brought in to be ruined; one of her favourite lodgers now lies in a cell.
GUILTY. Aged 36. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-218]
885. GEORGE BUDWORTH was indicted for stealing, on the 23rd of February, 1 walking-stick, value 3s., the goods of Joseph Barrett.
JOSEPH BARRETT. I live in Buckingham-road. I left this walking-stick in my shop about a fortnight before the 23rd of February; I know nothing of the prisoner.
JOHN MORRIS. I purchased this stick of the prisoner for 6d. on the 9th or 10th of February.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300415-219]
886. WILLIAM WHITE was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of April, 1 copper, value 30s., belonging to John Carver, then fixed to a building; 1 rug, value 2s.; 4 aprons, value 1s.; 1 shawl, value 1s.; 1 waistcoat, value 1s.; 1 shirt, value 1s.; 2 towels, value 6d., and 2 handkerchiefs, value 6d., the goods of the said John Carver , against the Statute, &c.
JOHN CARVER. I live at No. 5, Years'-court, Carey-street - I let part of the house. On the 9th of April, at six o'clock in the morning, I heard an unusual noise - I got up and found my street door open; I went to the back of my house, looked down the kitchen stairs, but seeing no doors open there, I returned, shut the street door, and went to bed - I got up at seven o'clock, went down stairs, and missed the linen from off the lines, a carpet, and a copper from the place where it had been fixed the night before; I missed four aprons, a shawl, a waistcoat, and the other articles stated - I cannot say how the parties got in; the door had been secure at one o'clock in the morning - this is the property.
THOMAS THOMPSON. I am a Police-officer. On the 9th of April, about twenty minutes before six o'clock in the morning, I was on duty in Great Queen-street, about three hundred yards from the prosecutor's, and saw the prisoner with something on his arm; I said to my partner,"Come here - here is something wrong;" the prisoner was then in Little Queen-street - I went and said, "What have you got here?" - he put this copper on my head and ran off; I sprung my rattle, and my partner took him - I found this linen in the copper.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. I suppose putting the copper on your head did not mend your sight very much? A. He tried to put it on my head to stop me; I think I had seen him for about three minutes - I think I had seen him once before; a man was taken up who was discharged - I do not think he was the prisoner's brother.
COURT. Q. Was the prisoner the man carrying the copper? A. Yes, he was.
JOHN URQUHART. I was with Thompson, and saw the prisoner carrying the copper.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you see the copper thrown over his head? A. Yes - I took the prisoner, but I hadlost him when he went into a house; I am quite sure he is the man - I took up two persons; it was because one jumped out of bed, and I took him - I said, "You are not the man I want, I want the other person;" he said, "There is no other person here," but I pulled off the rug and saw the prisoner with his stockings on, quite out of breath - this was in about three minutes after I lost sight of him; we took them both - the prisoner wanted to change coats with the man he called his brother; I said, "No, that is not your coat here is the white pocket hanging down."(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was in bed with my brother from eleven o'clock the night before; I was alarmed that morning by some one knocking at the door - my brother opened it; the officer came in, and said, "I think you are the person;" he then turned to the bed, saw me, and accused me because I was perspiring a little, as I am in the habit of doing.
GUILTY. Aged 19. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-220]
887. SARAH SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of April, 6 sovereigns and 11s., the monies of William Roberts, from his person.
WILLIAM ROBERTS. I am a German. I met the prisoner on the 11th of April; she asked me to go with her to Wentworth-street - I did not like to go into a house with her; it was about four o'clock in the morning - we went down a court, and she told me to give her a 1s.; I took out my purse, which had 12s. and six sovereigns in it, and gave her 1s. out of it - I put it into my pocket; we were on the ground together for about five minutes - I felt her take my purse from me; I said, "Give it back to me, I don't want to make a noise, and you shall keep 1l. if you will give me 5l. back;" two Policemen were going by - I said,"Now give me my money;" but she would not, and the Police took her.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. What did you give her? A. Two sixpences - she did put her arms round me; I went back with the Policeman to the yard where we had been - they found nothing on her the first time, but when I went back again, the Policeman found a sovereign in her hand; she forced me to go to the yard with her.
JOHN ROBINSON. I am a Police-officer. I was on duty in George-street, and met the prisoner and prosecutor - he said he had been robbed of six sovereigns and 11s.; the prisoner denied it, and said she had only 1s. 6d. about her, but on searching her I found 13s. and 1s. 1 1/2d. in copper - I am quite certain she denied having any money but 1s. 6d.; I went back to the spot with the prosecutor in hopes of finding the money, and when I returned I got permission to search the prisoner again - I opened the cellar-door and found a sovereign in her hand.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you search her at first attentively? A. No, sir, I was in hopes of finding the money, and I repaired back directly - it was dark; I found no money at the place the prosecutor said they had been at - the prisoner did at the latter end of my search request that a female might search her, but I had used no indecencies to her; when she asked that a woman might search her I left her and took her word - I did not say"Ding it to me and it will be all right;" the prosecutor pointed out the spot where he had been with her - my brother officer took it from her, but I saw it in her hand; the prosecutor did say he had lost a purse, but I saw none - I searched the cell, but found no sovereigns there.
COURT. Q. What was the distance from the spot they had been at, to the watch-house? A. About half a mile.
MICHAEL BERGIN. I was on duty at the watch-house when Robinson brought the prisoner in, a little after four o'clock in the morning; he searched her and found 13s. on her - he put that down, but did not search her very particularly; he was ordered to go back with the prosecutor to search where they had been, and see if he could find any gold - the prisoner positively denied that she had any more money about her; she was then locked up - when the prosecutor and Robinson returned, I was ordered to bring her up; I saw she was a little confused with her hands, and she asked for a drink of water - I said, "Let me see what is in your hand;" she opened her hand, and there was a sovereign.
Cross-examined. Q. Did the prosecutor tell you that he had told her she might keep one sovereign? A. No, not at that time - I was not present at the last search; I know nothing of the prosecutor.
Prisoner's Defence. I never saw any money except the 1s. he gave me; he held me from the time he charged me, till he gave me to the Police - I was used in a most inhuman manner, but I had no more money.
MR. PHILLIPS to ROBINSON. Q. You took her on a charge of robbing the prosecutor? A. Yes - she walked by my side, and I kept my eyes upon her; she had time to have opened the purse and taken a sovereign out, between the first search and the second - she was left in charge of the inspector, who is not here.
GUILTY. Aged 21. - Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18300415-221]
888. WILLIAM JAMES was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of February, 1 handkerchief, value 3s., the goods of Alexander Bailey Richmond, from his person.
ALEXANDER BAILEY RICHMOND. I am engaged at the periodical press. I was walking along the Strand with two friends, on the 27th of February - we turned up Catherine-street; it was about six o'clock in the evening - the Police-constable came and asked if any of us had lost our pocket-handkerchiefs; I felt, and said mine was gone - he requested us to wait, and, in about a minute and a half or two minutes, he brought the prisoner, and asked if I should know my handkerchief; I said Yes - we went to the watch-house, and I was asked if I had any mark on it; I said my family were very much in the habit of marking my things, and, on examining it, it had my mark and a number on it.
JAMES VICKERY. I was in Brydges-street, and saw the prisoner walking up Catherine-street, with two others; a gentleman desired me to look sharp after them - they were following the prosecutor and his two friends; I went on the other side, and in a few minutes I saw the prisoner with this handkerchief in his hand; I desired them to wait- I went and took this handkerchief from the prisoner's pocket.
Cross-examined by MR. HEATON. Q. What first attracted your attention to the prisoner? A. A gentlemantold me he thought they were very bad characters; I never saw the prisoner before - they were walking close behind the prosecutor; the prisoner said he had picked it up.
MR. RICHMOND. This is my handkerchief.
Cross-examined. Q. Had you used it just before? Yes - I am in the habit of carrying it in my hand, and, from the state of the evening, I am confident I must have used it within two or three minutes; it was not then in my hand, and it might possibly have dropped out of my pocket - I had had it in my hand in passing from the Sun newspaper-office; I cannot say how it left my person.
Prisoner's Defence. I was walking up Russell-street with two young men, and found the handkerchief - it was dirty; I took it up and put it into my pocket - I went down York-street, and as I returned, the officer took me and another young man; I was above two hundred yards from the place where they say this was committed, and it is not likely I should have returned.
JAMES VICKERY. I saw him with it his hand, not more than one yard from the prosecutor.
GUILTY. Aged 17. - Confined Six Months.
Recommended to Mercy by the Prosecutor.
