938. CHARLES PRICE was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of April, 1 tea-chest, value 18s., the goods of George Gairdner.
GEORGE GAIRDNER. I am an upholsterer, and live in Tottenham-court-road. This tea-chest was standing on a chest of drawers in my shop, on Saturday, the 10th of April - it was safe at nine o'clock in the morning when I left.
JOHN MURRAY. I saw the prisoner near the shop; he went and sat on a sofa in front of the shop; he put his hand into the shop, took the tea-chest, put it under his jacket, and went off - I ran across; there was another man with the prisoner - I could not hear what they said; the other beckoned the prisoner and they turned down Howland-street - I did not lose sight of the prisoner.
WILLIAM GODFREY. I saw the prisoner take the chest from the shop - he sat on the sofa, put his hand back, and took it; I followed and took him with it in his possession; he ran down Howland-street.
HENRY RICHARD BUCKERIDGE. I received charge of the chest.
Prisoner's Defence. I saw that on the step of a door of a public-house - I took it up and asked if it belonged to them; then this gentleman said I must go with him.
GUILTY. Aged 21. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-274]
939. JAMES McDERMOTT was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of September, 2 pairs of trousers, value 7s.; 1 coat, value 5s.; 1 shirt, value 2s.; 1 hat, value 3s.; 2 waistcoats, value 2s.; 1 pair of stockings, value 1s.; 1 shawl, value 2s., the goods of Michael Collins; and I pair of trousers, value 4s.; 2 shirts, value 4s.; 1 gown, value 5s.; 3 handkerchiefs, value 3s.; and 1 towel, value 6d., the goods of John Tattersal.
MICHAEL COLLINS. I am a labourer. On the 29th of September I saw the prisoner at the Castle, in the Broadway; he came and sat down close against me; my clothes were then in my box up stairs, as I lodged at that house - all the articles stated were in my box except my hat, and that was gone likewise, and an old hat left in its place which I knew was the prisoner's; the prisoner was gone when I missed them, and I have seen none of them since -Tattersal's things were in my box in my care.
Prisoner. Q. What intercourse with me had you, that you knew the hat was mine? A. Because you wore it on the watch with me, and you had it on that morning - I took particular notice of it; I could swear to it from all the hats I ever saw in my life - it was at nine o'clock when you were first in company with me that morning; afterwards two other men came in, and Johnson and they went to get their breakfasts, I believe.
JOHN WOODS. On the 29th of September I was standing on the Broadway pavement, and saw the prisoner going from towards the house where Collins lodged to his own lodging, it was about half-past eleven o'clock; he had a very large bundle with him tied up in a red shawl, which I have seen Collins wear round his neck; I saw about his trousers a parcel, which appeared about the size of a man's arm, and part of a gown tail hung over his boot - he saw it as he knocked at the door, and tucked it in.
Prisoner. Q. Was not that the time you ought to have stopped such a man? Witness. I ought to have done it, and you are the man.
Prisoner. He is a man who ought not to be allowed to give evidence; he would come home at all hours of the night, and has a wife and child he is bringing up in a bad manner.
PHILIP ANDREWS. I had a warrant against the prisoner on the 7th of April on another charge; on his examination at Bow-street I heard of this - I went to the Castle, and found Collins had removed; on Good Friday I went to his lodgings, where he gave me a list of what he had lost, and this letter which the prisoner had sent him - (read.)
Friend Collins. I met to-day Johnson, and he told me that I have done something wrong in your place, which if I have I am not conscious of so doing; however, he advised me to come to you, as he was going out of town for a few days, but you may depend on it you shall be satisfied, if I have done any mischief, before three o'clock on Monday next, and I have requested of Johnson to call on you when he comes to town, hoping this will satisfy you till then; I am living here in Camberwell with a pensioner of my own regiment, as I expect to receive prizemoney every day, and should I be so fortunate as to get it before Monday you shall hear from me, as I gave Johnson up my prizeticket this morning when I met him in St. Martin's-lane - he desired me to tell you of it, and to write this letter to you; I hope you will make yourself easy till you see him or me, between this and Monday. No more at present. JAMES McDERMOTT.
I hope you do not mean to hurt me till you see me.
Prisoner's Defence. I was drinking with Collins on account of the watch being broken up at the time; three or four more were in company with us; some time in the day he got tipsy, and left the company - I went to my own quarters in Westminster, and next day I went down to Chatham to draw some prize-money; in going I met Johnson, who told me Collins had been robbed, and had a suspicion I had done it; I wrote the letter, and stated as you have heard.
GUILTY. Aged 38. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-275]
940. MARY KING was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of April, 2 gowns, value 15s.; 1 frock, value 2s.; 1 handkerchief, value 1s., and 1 pinafore, value 1s., the goods of John Parks.
SARAH PARKS. I am the wife of John Parks - he lives in Somers'-town. I lost these articles while I was out at work; I was sent for to the watch-house, and saw them there; I know nothing of the prisoner.
CHARLES DENNIS. I am a silversmith. On Wednesday last I saw the prisoner watching some children at play at a door - I took notice of her; the children went a few doors off - the prisoner stepped into the prosecutor's house, and came out with these gowns in a basket, and these other things wrapped up in this pinafore - she began running; I asked one child whether she knew the prisoner- she said No; I pursued, and took her.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that she had come from Southampton in search of her daughter, who had been seduced, and that on going into the prosecutrix's house she was tempted to take the property, being without a shilling.
GUILTY. Aged 40.
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury. - Confined 1 Month.
[trialAccount t18300415-276]
941. THOMAS JONES was indicted for stealing, on the 22nd of February, 1 saw, value 4s., and 1 plane, value 4s., the goods of William Barton.
WILLIAM BARTON. I am a carpenter. On the 22nd of February I worked till breakfast - I then went away, and left my saw and plane in a house in Kingsland-road - I had to go to another place; I came back next morning, and missed this saw and plane.
CHARLES WALLER. I took the prisoner on the 22nd of February, in Hoxton New-town, with this plane.
EDMUND JULIUS SUTTON. I am an apprentice to a pawnbroker. This saw was pawned on the 22nd of February, between seven and eight o'clock, by the prisoner, I believe.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I keep a stall in the street, and sell bells, locks, and other things - a man brought these things, and sold them to me for 5s.; I wanted some money, and pawned the saw - I then took the plane to another pawnbroker; I said it was my own, because I thought they would let me go.
GUILTY. Aged 21. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-277]
942. JOHN JACOBS was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of March, 1 coat, value 10s.; 1 waistcoat, value 2s.; 1 pair of trousers, value 5s., and 1 hat, value 2s., the goods of John Robinson.
JOHN ROBINSON. I am a warehouseman. The prisoner and another man agreed to purchase these things for 30s.; the prisoner had but 5s. - he said, "I have a cheque, I will get the change, then come, and pay you;" I staid three hours - he did not come, and I told my servant not to let the things go without their paying the 25s.; I went out,and when I returned in the evening the prisoner was in custody - I live at Paddington.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Was not that a pretty good sum? A. I do not know; it was not the other man who bought them, but the prisoner.
SOPHIA MEEKING. I am in the prosecutor's employ. The prisoner and the other man came and looked over the things - the other man said, "Put them into your bag, and go;" the prisoner did so, the other pulled off his own coat, and fitted on another - he then said he had no money; I said I could not let the clothes go without - I pushed past the other man to go after the prisoner; the other prevented me, and held the gate to - I snatched it out of his hand, and ran to the top of the street; the prisoner set off running -I called Stop thief! he was pursued, and stopped - I never lost sight of him; I asked him to give me the clothes, and after some persuasion he gave me them, and followed me back to the house.
Cross-examined. Q. Then he might have ran away? A. No; the officer was close behind him - I was not present at the first bargain about them; I had not made a new bargain with the other man to let him have them for 25s., nor consented that the prisoner should have one of the articles for the 5s.; he was taken to the office - he was not allowed to offer the 25s. to me.
JABEZ WOOD. I saw the witness running after the prisoner, and I stopped him - he did not say any thing.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 19. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18300415-278]
943. WILLIAM COOKE was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of February, 1 pocket-book, value 5s., the goods of Myer Nathan, from his person.
MYER NATHAN. I was in Hart-street, Covent-garden, on the 15th of February, about four o'clock in the afternoon, and my pocket-book was extracted from my pocket- I turned round, and saw the prisoner running; I ran, and overtook him without losing sight of him - my pocketbook was handed to me from a cellar.
JOHN WILLIAM LLOYD. I was going through Covent-garden market. I saw the prosecutor and the prisoner walking very close behind him - I heard the prosecutor cry Stop thief! I turned round, and the prisoner passed me - I made an attempt to catch him, but missed him; I saw the pocket-book then in his hand - he stooped down, and threw it into a cellar; I saw it handed up very shortly afterwards, and the prosecutor claimed it.
Prisoner. I leave it to the Jury.
GUILTY. Aged 25. - Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-279]
First Middlesex Jury - Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
944. HENRY WILSON was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of February, 25 handkerchiefs, value 7l., 3 pairs of socks, value 2s., and 3 slippers, value 3s., the goods of Margaret Dixon and another, in the dwelling-house of Mary Clark and another.
ELIZABETH CLARK. I am Mary Clark's sister-in-law This happened at a public-house; the landlord died on the 5th of January - Mary Clark and his brother then had charge of the house, which is at Knightsbridge; Mary Clark 's husband's name is Thomas - this property belonged to a person who had come in; I did not know it was stolen till afterwards.
JOHN ROYLE. I am porter to Margaret Dixon, who lives in Cockspur-street. I had this property in a parcel to take to Sloane-street, on the 20th of February - I went into the Queen's Head, at Knightsbridge, about two o'clock; I sat in the tap-room, nearly three quarters of an hour, with the parcel in my possession; I then went to the bar to speak to Clarke; I returned in two minutes, and the parcel was gone from the table where I had left it; I could hear nothing of it in the house - I cannot swear the prisoner was there; this was Saturday, and on the Tuesday I found it at a pawnbroker's.
WILLIAM CHURCH. I am an officer. On the 22nd, in consequence of information, I went to the prisoner's lodgings, and found this duplicate under a chimney ornament, on the mantel-piece - he was then in custody; I knew he lodged there, for I saw him there on Sunday, the 21st, about another case - he wasthen with me.
JOHN KILLINGWORTH. I am servant to Mr. Perkins, pawnbroker, of King's-road, Chelsea. I have a piece of silk pawned for 12s. on the 20th of February - I have a recollection of having seen the prisoner before, but am not quite positive he is the man; this duplicate is what I gave the person.
JOHN BUCKERIDGE. I am in the employ of Bell and Co., silk-manufacturers. I know this silk to be their make, and believe it to be part of the goods sent to Dixon and Evans from our house; this invoice is what I sent with the goods.
JOHN ROYLE. I have every reason to believe this to be part of the contents of the parcel; it had come for Bell and Co. - I lost the parcel about a quarter to three o'clock.
WILLIAM JAMES WILSON. On Saturday, the 20th of February, the prisoner came to my master's shop in Piccadilly, for some black varnish - I am a brass-finisher; he took off his hat and took a piece of black silk from it- I asked him what it was for; he said for a handkerchief, and said I should have one for 5s. - I said I did not want it; he put it into his hat again - I knew him before; he was groom to Mr. Murray; it was about four o'clock.
Prisoner's Defence. Mr. Murray pays us at this public-house, and we run a weekly score there; I had a post of half-and-half there that day from our foreman - I had a black silk handkerchief in my hut, with two 1d. loaves and a piece of cheese; my hat fell off in the street - I picked up the handkerchief at the time.
GUILTY (of stealing only.) Aged 20.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-280]
Before Mr. Justice Gaselee.
945. ROBERT BENNETT and STEPHEN GRAVES were indicted for burglariously breaking and entering the dwelling-house of Henry Hyndman, about twelve o'clock on the night of the 21st of March, with intent to steal.
HENRY HYNDMAN. I am a Parliamentary agent, and live in Fludyer-street, Westminster. Bennett came into my service as a clerk upwards of two years ago, and continued with me about five weeks.
JOSEPH CRANE. I am a private constable of the Police.I was on duty in Fludyer-street on the 21st of March; I came off duty at twelve o'clock and observed a man in King-street, opposite Fludyer-street, and suspecting something was wrong, I walked to the end of King-street, and saw Cole, a Police-constable, in Charles-street - I told him, then came back, and saw the same man opposite Fludyer-street; I went up Fludyer-street on the right side, and in turning back again on the left hand I heard a noise in the prosecutor's area and saw the two prisoners in the area against the window - they saw me and drew back; I sprung my rattle - Graves came up; I asked what he did there - he said some person had thrown his hat over into the area; I said I must take him into custody - he used very rough language; we had a struggle for a few minutes - he called to the other prisoner to came up, but he did not, and with the assistance of another man we got him over the rails; in the mean time Cole came up - I charged him with the man in the area, and with assistance I took Graves to the watch-house; on his person I found a centrebit and a broken knife; I returned and found Bennett in Cole's custody.
JOHN COLE. I am a Police-constable. I was on duty in Charles-street between twelve and one o'clock; I heard a noise in Fludyer-street, and proceeded to the spot; I found Crane tussling with Graves - Crane said he had come out of the area, and that there was another man in the area; I got a light, went down into the area, and found Bennett in the coal-hole - I asked him what he did there; he said some one had thrown his hat over - I found a skeleton-key on his person; in the corner where he was concealed there were several other keys, a chisel, a small crow-bar, the handle of a centre-bit, and several other skeleton-keys, which I gave up to Prior.
Cross-examined by Mr. HEATON. Q. He made no resistance? A. No, he seemed very much dejected.
JOSEPH PRIOR. On Thursday night I went to Fludyer-street and found Bennett in custody; I went down the area - the kitchen window was lifted up, the pannel of the inside shutter bored throught with a centre-bit, and this piece and this small piece of the pannel were lying in the area; I fitted them into the pannel - I afterwards got the centre-bit and fitted it to some of the holes which had been bored; they agreed with it - after going inside the kitchen this blade of a knife was picked up, in my presence, by an officer, who is not here; I have compared it with the handle found upon Graves - they correspond exactly; the blade had been put through the hole, and I suppose snapped off, and fell inside - the impression on the shutter exactly corresponded with the crow-bar; the space between the inside of the window and the shutter I should suppose is not above two or three inches.
Cross-examined. Q. Is the kitchen under the house? A. Yes; the coal-hole is under the street - I did not find the centre-bit.
Prisoner Graves. Q. Where was the blade of the knife found? A. On the ledge under the window; I never said it was found on the table - I said I was near the table when it was picked up.
MR. HYNDMAN. I was in bed, and was alarmed by the Police knocking at my door; I believe I went to bed last- I make a practice of going round my house before I go to bed; the shutters were fastened with a bar - the window sash, I believe was not fastened; there is a catch to it, but it is so stiff it is useless - I have no doubt the sash was down that night, but I did not examine that; it is the cook's business to fasten the window - she is not here; it might have been partly up for what I know.
MR. HEATON. Q. At what hour did you go round the house? A. I think about half-past eleven o'clock - I had a light in my hand, and can say the shutters were whole; I distinctly remember there was no appearance of violence on the shutter.
NOT GUILTY.
The prisoners were detained, to be indicted for the misdemeanor.
[trialAccount t18300415-281]
Before Mr. Justice Park.
946. ELIZABETH BOYD was indicted for breaking and entering the dwelling-house of Mary Townsend, on the 11th of April, and stealing 1 jacket, value 5s.; 1 pair of trousers, value 4s., and 1 pair of drawers, value 6d., the goods of James Bills; 1 shifts, value 6d., and 1 petticoat, value 6d., the goods of Mary Townsend.
JAMES BILLS. On Saturday night, the 10th of April, between twelve and one o'clock, I met Mary Townsend in Tothill-street, and went home with her; I saw her fasten the door - I staid all night with her; I awoke about half-past five o'clock, and missed my things - she was in bed.
MARY TOWNSEND. I live in Union-court, Orchard-street. I took this man home, and locked the door - I only occupy one room; the landlady does not live in the house- I was awoke in the morning by Bills, and missed my shift and petticoat; I found the door open - Bills was making a noise about his things; Wilkins, the Policeman, came up to know if we had lost any thing - the staple of the lock was not forced; I do not know how it was opened.
Prisoner. Q. Did you not give me that bundle? A. No, on my oath I did not; you had not taken me home drunk on the Wednesday.
JOSEPH WILKINS. I am a Policeman. About half-past five o'clock in the morning, I saw the prisoner cross Orchard-street with a bundle, and stopped her; she said they were her own things, and finding they were men's clothes I took her to the watch-house; I found the witnesses, who owned them - I lent the prosecutor a coat; he went to the watch-house, and claimed them in the prisoner's presence.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I met that woman at the end of the court, as I was going home; she asked me to be so kind as to take these things to my lodging, saying a man had left them with her, as he had no money - I had taken her home drunk a few days before, and she burst her door open.
MARY TOWNSEND re-examined. It is false; I did not give it her - there were no marks of violence on my door, but I am positive I locked it.
JAMES BILLS. I did not examine the lock.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300415-282]
Before Mr. Justice Gaselee.
947. MARY BELL was indicted for breaking and entering the dwelling-house of Edward Roberts, on the 14thof April, and stealing 1 apron, value 1s.; 1 cap, value 2s., and 1 pair of scissors, value 6d., his property.
SARAH ROBERTS. I am the wife of Edward Roberts - we live in Nottingham-court, on the second floor, and have but one room; the landlord does not live in the house. On the 14th of April I went out about half-past nine o'clock in the morning, and locked the door; I returned about half-past ten, and found it broken open - every thing was in confusion, and I missed these things; I had left the scissors on the table - I do not know the prisoner.
MARY KENNEDY. I live in the kitchen of this house. I met the prisoner coming down stairs that morning, rather after half-past nine o'clock - she bade me good morning; I never saw her before, but am sure she is the woman- she went out, and I took no farther notice till Mrs. Roberts came in and missed her things; I then told her, and at half-past four o'clock I met the prisoner in Short's-gardens, and detained her.
SARAH ROBERTS. I went out with Kennedy to look for the prisoner, and at half-past four o'clock saw her, and gave her in charge to Wadey, who found my scissors on her, but nothing else - she said she had bought them for 1 1/2d., but I swear to them.
JAMES WADEY. Roberts gave the prisoner into my charge in Short's-gardens, for breaking into her room and robbing her - she denied it; I found a pair of scissors on her, which she said she gave 1 1/2d. for - she did not say when.
SARAH ROBERTS. I had these scissors ground and new rivetted about a month ago, and had a point made to cut button-holes, as I do slop work; I have had them above two years.
Prisoner's Defence. I am sorry I bought them if they were stolen - if I had liberty I could bring the woman forward who I bought them of; I do not know her name, but know her by sight.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300415-283]
Before Mr. Justice Gaselee.
948. THOMAS SKUCE was indicted for breaking and entering the dwelling-house of Jane Hills, on the 31st of March, and stealing 1 box, value 1d., and 6lbs. of iron, value 4d., her property.
JANE HILLS. I rent two rooms in Half Nichol-street, Bethnal-green, and also hire a cellar - the landlord does not live in the house. On the 28th of March I saw this box of old iron in my cellar - the cellar is divided into two parts; I have the back part, the other is not used - my part was locked, but there is a hole from the front cellar which a boy could creep through from the yard; he could then open a door in my cellar, which is neither locked nor bolted; it is merely boards which form part of the partition.
GEORGE ALLCOCK. I was an officer when this occurred. I now live at Bethnal-green. On the 31st of March I was in Half Nichol-street about half-past eight o'clock, and saw a boy coming along with something under his arm; I went over and found it was this box covered over - in consequence of what he told me, I went to the next house to the prosecutrix, where the prisoner's mother lives, and found the prisoner there; I asked if he gave his brother that box to sell - he voluntarily said that he did; I asked where he got it - he said out of the prosecutrix's house; he is older than his brother who I had met with the box; it contains old iron, and is worth 5d.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I found it in my mother's cellar.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY (of stealing only.) Aged 16.
Whipped and Discharged.
First London Jury - Before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18300415-284]
949. JOHN CARLISLE was indicted for unlawfully procuring 12 counterfeit shillings, with intent to utter the same.
MESSRS. SCARLETT and ELLIS conducted the prosecution.
GEORGE STARR. I keep the Cross Keys public-house, Wood-street, Cheapside. On the 6th of March, about seven o'clock in the evening, the prisoner came in and asked for a glass of spruce and the newspaper - I gave him the spruce, and directed him to the coffee-room to read the paper; he gave me a shilling in payment, I gave him a sixpence and 4d. - I afterwards found it was a bad shilling; he was then gone - I thought he was in the coffee-room, but he had not gone there at all; I threw the shilling, into the fire, and it melted down like lead almost immediately - on Saturday, the 13th of March, he came about the same hour, and asked for a glass of spruce and the newspaper; I left my man to serve him - I recognized him and said, "You are the very man who gave me the bad shilling last week;" he then offered me a good one for it - he had not paid me any thing that night; I called in the constable who took him - he paid a good half-crown for what he had, and as the constable took him into the room he dropped a shilling, which I marked and gave to Earl, observing that it was a bad one; I am certain it dropped from him.
FRANCIS EARL. I am a constable of Cripplegate. I received the prisoner in charge about seven o'clock in the evening - as I took him from the bar to the parlour, to search him more conveniently, a shilling dropped on the ground; I have every reason to believe it fell from him -I found in his waistcoat pocket twelve counterfeit shillings and four good sixpences, wrapped in a piece of rag and then in paper.
JOHN FIELD. I am an inspector of the Mint. The shilling produced is counterfeit, it is Britannia metal, not worth a farthing; the twelve others are also counterfeit, and cast in the same mould - Britannia metal weighs rather more than one-fourth less than silver.
The prisoner pleaded distress.
GUILTY. Aged 18. - Confined Six Months.

[trialAccount t18300415-288]
953. THOMAS HACKWELL was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of March, 5 sovereigns, and 15 shillings, the monies of William Dowson.
WILLIAM DOWSON. I am a sadler. The prisoner lodged in my house - my daughter gave him a cheque for 5l. 15s. on the 11th of March, 1829, to get the money; he did not bring the money.
JEMIMA DOWSON. I gave the prisoner this cheque(read, drawn by D. Fletcher, on Messrs, Gillespie for 5l. 15s.) - I gave it to him to be at Messrs. Gillespie's by ten o'clock; I told him if he would go, and come back directly, I would give him something to get a breakfast- he did not come back; I did not see him again till last March - his wife was out at the time; he went away with his child - she returned in a short time, took all their things, and went away.
WILLIAM COCKERILL. I was at Messrs. Gillespie's when the cheque was paid - I entered it in the book, but I cannot tell who had it; it was paid on the day it is entered - Mr. Dowson came in the afternoon, and asked if we had paid the money; it had been paid two hours before.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. If you had not looked at the book, would you have remembered any thing about it? A. I distinctly remember the payment, but not the person; I looked at the back to see if it was entered - Mr. Gillespie asked me the question, and mentioned the name of Dowson; I cannot say whether it was a man or woman received it.
COURT. Q. Can you be sure whether the person who complained that the money had not been paid, was the person who presented it? A. My impression is that it was not, but I cannot be positive - it was paid in five sovereigns, but whether the silver was in shillings or halfcrowns I do not know; we keep sovereigns exclusively for the purpose of paying these orders, and never pay notes.
Q. But if I wanted a 5l. note to send in a letter, you would accommodate me with it? A. It is a very unusual thing; I never knew it done.
JURY to WILLIAM DOWSON. Q. What lodging had the prisoner of you? A. A ready furnished room down the yard; there were seven or eight weeks' rent due at this time.
GUILTY. Aged 39. - Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18300415-289]
954. PHILIP HUTCHINSON was indicted for bigamy.
GEORGE JACKSON GREEN. I saw the prisoner married to a person of the name of Stubbs, about twelve years ago, at Bromley St. Leonard's, but the clerk is not here; I was sexton and am so still - James Brand was also present, but he is dead; there are no other witnesses here.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300415-290]
955. JOHN GRIMES was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of March, 1 ham, value 7s., the goods of Thomas Courtney.
THOMAS COURTNEY. I am a cheesemonger, and live in Drury-lane. On the 20th of March I lost a ham, about half-past eleven o'clock at night - I did not see it taken, but the Policeman brought it back, and I believe it to be mine; I had had it in my hand twice that day - I do not see any difference between this and mine; it is the same weight and size.
JOHN RAWLEY. I stopped the prisoner on suspicion, three or four hundred yards from the shop, with this ham under his arm - I asked where he got it; he said at a shop leading to Clare-market, which is the prosecutor's - I lodged him in the watch-house, and took the ham to the shop; he said he bought it at 5 1/2d. per lb., and it weighed 10 1/4lbs., but it weighs 12 1/4lbs. - he was going from the shop.
Prisoner. I said 6d. per pound.
THOMAS COURTNEY. It would have been 7d. per pound.
Prisoner's Defence. I saw this ham and asked the price - they said 7d. per pound; I said I would give 6d. for the whole, and it was weighed to me - it weighed 10 1/2lbs., and I gave 5s. 1 1/2d. for it.
JURY to JOHN RAWLEY. Q. Had he any money on him? A. He stated that he had none, but I believe he was not searched.
GUILTY. Aged 21. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-291]
Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
956. JOHN GREEN was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of April, 8 live tame fowls, price 16s., and I live tame duck, price 4s., the property of Edward Sadler.
ROBERT BATEMAN. I am a Police-officer. On the 7th of April, at a quarter before five o'clock in the morning, I met the prisoner on Stepney-green, with this duck wrapped up in a smock frock - I asked what he had got; he told me nothing - I found this duck, and then he said he had bought it half an hour before, of a sailor for 1s. 6d.; I found nothing else on him.
MARY ANN BRUNEGAS. Mr. Edward Sadler lives at Laytonstone - I take care of his place, and had the fowls and duck in a hen-house; they were all safe and locked up on the 6th of April, and when I got up the next morning they were all gone - I missed eight fowls and this Muscovy duck; I know nothing of the prisoner - they had taken the tiles off, and got in at the top of the house; I know this duck very well, and he knows me.
Prisoner's Defence. I had it tied up in my old smockfrock - I said it was a Muscovy duck I gave 1s. 6d. for.
GUILTY. Aged 17. - Transported for Seven Years.

WILLIAM WATERER. On the 4th of April I was in Catherine-street, and saw a gentleman, who gave his name as John Williams, at the watch-house, but it was a false address; he is not here.

959. ELIZABETH GOODALL was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of April, 3 sovereigns, and 1 half-sovereign, the monies of Samuel Stewart, from his person.
SAMUEL STEWART, I am an Excise-officer. I was in Shadwell High-street between one and two o'clock in the morning of the 10th of April - I was out on business; the prisoner accosted me in the street, and asked me to go home with her - I refused, and desired her to go about her business; she caught hold of my right arm, put her hand into my right-hand waistcoat pocket, and took out three sovereigns and a half, which I had had safe five minutes before she came up to me - I had no hole in the pocket; there was another woman in company, who walked on my left-hand side - she did not take any thing from me; the prisoner came up in about ten minutes, and took my arm - I did not stop at all with them, and I swear the prisoner is the woman who took my money; she was taken in three or four minutes, but nothing was found on her - I had been drinking a glass, but was as sober as I am now; I had been to Shadwell High-street, and was going home sober.
Prisoner. He neither knew when, nor where, nor how he lost his money. Witness. Yes, I do; the Policeman was near, and I called him to follow her.
DAVID LOVICK. I am a Police-officer. The prosecutor accused this woman of robbing him of three sovereigns and a half - he was not sober, but appeared to know what he was about; I pursued the prisoner - she had just turned round Cannon-street; I called her, and she turned back - I took her in front of the prosecutor, and found on her 1s. 6 1/2d.; there was another woman with the prosecutor- I saw them all three standing together near the watering-house; I went up to Bluegate-fields, dispersed some girls, and when I returned the prosecutor stated the case to me - I took the prisoner; the prosecutor was reeling about, but was able to answer questions.
Prisoner's Defence. I met the man with a woman holding his arm - the woman said, "Come," and I went with them to the night-house; he called for a quartern of gin; he said, "I will go inside" - I bade him good night, crossed the road, and went down Cannon-street, when the officer took me.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300415-295]
960. WILLIAM FLOWER was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of March, 1 handkerchief, value 4s., the goods of Robert Harman, from his person.
ROBERT HARMAN. On the 1st of March, between five and six o'clock in the afternoon, I was in Coventry-street Haymarket; I had a handkerchief in my pocket - I did not feel any thing, but a gentleman told me my pocket was picked; I turned, saw the prisoner in his hand, and my handkerchief at his feet.
Cross-examined by MR. HEATON. Q. Is that gentleman here? A. Yes; this is the handkerchief - I had not seen the prisoner before.
THOMAS HOLLINS. I was in the street, and saw the prisoner and other following the gentleman - I watched them, and saw the prisoner pick his pocket; I collared him, and the gentleman turned and took him.
Cross-examined. Q. Do you swear you saw him take it out of the pocket? A. Yes; the others were behind him - I do not know what became of them.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. This gentleman says there were three of us, but there was only one more beside me; I saw another boy take it - I was going to tell the gentleman, when I was accused of it.
GUILTY. Aged 17. - Transported for Life.

961. PETER STUBBS was indicted for stealing, on the 22nd of February, 156 finger plates, value 5l., and 55 locks, value 4l., the goods of Thomas Frith; and that he had been previously convicted of felony.
MR. BARRY conducted the prosecution.
JONATHAN MOORE. I am a servant to Mr. Thomas Frith; he is a block ironmonger, and lives in High Holborn. On the 22nd of February the prisoner came for a pair of butt hinges - he told me to make the holes larger; he was walking about the shop - I watched him, and saw him go to the partition where the finger-plates were kept in parcels, which are marked; he took a parcel, and put it under his apron - my master's son was in the shop; we went to him, found the plates under his arm, and my master's son took them from him - I saw him take them from the partition; we have lost a great many of these articles.
Prisoner. He found nothing on me at all. Witness. Yes, I did, under his apron.
BENJAMIN BIRDSEYE REEVE. I am the pawnbroker. I have a great number of plates, pawned by the prisoner at different times.
THOMAS JOHN BARNETT FRITH. I am the prosecutor's son. I was in the shop, and saw the prisoner stoop down, and take the parcel - he passed it from his right hand to his left; I went, and took it from him - this is it; our mark is on the paper.
Prisoner's Defence. When I went to the place, where these parcels were, I took up one of them, and the witness said, "He had got a parcel in his hand," and called his father down stairs. GUILTY.
MATTHEW CARTER. I produce a certificate of the conviction of the prisoner, by the name of Peter Stokes, on the 9th of July, in the 9th year of his Majesty's reign; he was sentenced to three months imprisonment - I took him, and know he is the man.

963. JAMES WATSON was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of March, 8 books, value 4s., the goods of William Myers, his master.
WILLIAM MYERS. I am bookseller and stationer, andlive in the Commercial-road - the prisoner was my apprentice for about twelve months; these books were found in his possession - he was attempting to sell them; I know they are mine - they are Sunday-school poems.
GEORGE DEVERELL. I am beadle of Shadwell. On the morning of the 10th of March, I was going along High-street, Shadwell, and saw the prisoner with something under his jacket - I went into the shop he came out of, and found he had been offering books for sale; I then went to an opposite house and looked through the window - I watched him for some time, and saw him go into a shop; I sent a person, and he was brought to me - he said he was going to sell them for his mother, who had a stock of them, and he was selling some for a breakfast; I took him to the watch-house, and sent for the prosecutor.
Prisoner. My master used to go and stay out all hours of the night; he kept me at home at work on Sunday, instead of going to church, and gave me no money.
WILLIAM MYERS. I gave him money to go and see his mother, and he never went - I used to send him to church every Sunday with my sons.
GUILTY. Aged 16. - Whipped and Discharged.

964. JAMES FRY was indicted for stealing, on the 2nd of January, 11 sovereigns, the monies of John Meredith, his master.
JOHN MEREDITH. The prisoner was my servant. On the 2nd of January my wife sent him with eleven sovereigns to my brother.
WILLIAM MEREDITH. The prisoner came and brought me eleven sovereigns in a paper parcel; I saw the parcel, but not the money - I told him to take it back and to tell my brother I was not going in the City to-day, and he had better pay it himself.
Cross-examined by MR. CRESWELL. Q. Then he delivered you a parcel, which you suppose contained the money, and as far as that went, completed his errand? A. Yes; I did not take it out of his hand - I went before the Magistrate; I heard what the prisoner said - I think it was taken down in writing; I did not say I would save his neck, but would transport him for life - I might say he deserved transporation.
MARY ANN MEREDITH. I am the prosecutor's wife. I put eleven sovereigns into a silk purse, tied that in a brown paper, and sent the prisoner to William Meredith - I told him to say I was very unwell, or I would have brought it myself; I never saw him again till I saw him in a green-grocer's shop, in East-street, on the 26th of March, and had him taken.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you go to his mother's? A. Yes; he had been there, but was out - I have never seen the money.
Prisoner. As I was returning I got into a crowd; my pocket was picked, and I was ashamed to return.
GUILTY. Aged 18. - Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-300]
965. WILLIAM HILLIER PERRING was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of February, 2 quarts of wine, value 8s.; 1 quart of rum, value 3s., and 3 bottles, value 6d., the goods of Robert Brown.
MR. ADOLPHUS conducted the prosecution.
ROBERT BROWN. I keep the Berwick Arms, at the corner of Castle-street, Oxford-street . About Christmas my cellar was under repair; I employed Godfrey, and he employed the prisoner, who I believe is his son-in-law - the door was taken off and repaired by the prisoner; in that cellar there was wine to a considerable amount - it is the inner cellar; it opens into the spirit cellar - I have examined the partition between the two cellars, and find a hole cut on one side of the post, for a bottle to pass and re-pass, and a mark where some one's finger had been cut and bled; I never allowed the prisoner to go to my cellar after the job was over - he never asked; on Saturday night, the 27th of February, my servant gave me information, and I had the prisoner taken on the 1st of March.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. When did you find the blood? A. On the Monday morning, when I found three bottles broken, and lying in the cellar; I do not know how long it had been there - the prisoner was taken on the same morning; I did not order his hands and wrists to be searched - the officer mentioned the blood to me.
HANNAH HOARE. I am the prosecutor's servant, On the Saturday I saw the prisoner come from the cellar - I followed him up stairs into the tap-room, where he had been in the whole of the day; I do not think he had been at work for a day or two.
WILLIAM TAYLOR. I am a porter and chairman. On the 27th of February I was with the prisoner at the prosecutor's house, waiting to be paid; my father and other persons were there - the prisoner beckoned me out, I went out of the room: he said, "Don't take any notice, but it is my birth-day; and if you will go with me I will give you a glass of wine;" I said, "We had better go back, and pay that one pot between us;" he said we should not be five minutes: I went with him up to a house, in Castle-court, where Lousia Lawrence lodges - when we got there, he took a bottle of wine out of his pocket, and we drank it; I think I had two glasses: he then pulled out a bottle of rum - Lousia's mother said, "This won't do:" the prisoner and I had a glass a piece; I do not think there was much drank - the bottle and the rest of it was left there: we then went to Mr. Brown's, and paid a pint a piece towards the pot we had - I thought I had been treated enough and did not expect to have a second edition of it; but when we were coming out: he said, "I have another bottle of wine at home, suppose we drink it at your lodging" - we went to his father-in-law's to get it: I did not go in, but he did - we went to Mr. Brown's to get a light: he then went to a fishmonger's-shop, and he served out the wine among a number of people there; we then went home and drank the rest.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you see him take any wine from Mr. Brown's? A. No; Godfrey is a carpenter - he bore a fair honest character.
COURT. Q. How long have you known him? A. I suppose nine or ten years; he has borne a good character as a tradesman, as far as I know - there was one time I heard he had been here, but I was not in Court, and did not know much of him then: I have only heard of it since this business.
LOUSIA LAWRENCE. The prisoner came to my lodging on the 27th of February, and brought a bottle with wine and a bottle with about half a pint of rum in it; we drank the wine amongst us, and I believe two glasses of rumwere drank by Taylor and him - there was but a little left; the officer came and asked for two bottles: my mother gave him the bottles, they were clean.
Cross-examined. Q. Would you venture to swear they were the two bottles the prisoner brought? A. No; the prisoner and the witness were very much in liquor - the bottle was not one third full of rum; I suppose it was between a pint and a quart.
MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. Were they not what are commonly called quart bottles? A. I cannot answer.
COURT. Q. What is your age? A. Twenty-one; I am a waistcoat-maker - I cannot tell whether both the bottles were alike; I drank part of their contents - it was poured into a glass: it was candle-light - it was dark coloured wine; I am not acquainted with wine - there was rum, not gin in the other bottle.
HENRY RICHARD BUCKERIDGE. I took the prisoner at his father's, in company with Whaley; and in his righthand pocket I found this key; I took him to Mr. Brown's, and left him in the parlour while I went down - I found this key would open the spirit-cellar door rather better than Mr. Brown's key would; this is the key - I went to Lawrence's lodgings with Taylor; I asked if he had left any wine or bottles there - Lawrence's mother put her hand out of the window and gave me them out of a gutter; they had been fresh washed: I did not search the lodging; I had been to Taylor's lodgings, and he gave me a bottle which had contained Port wine.
Cross-examined. Q. Did not he say his father-in-law, Godfrey had given it him? A. No. I saw the blood in the cellar; it might have been there a day or two - I examined the prisoner's arms and wrists, but found no cut on them.
JURY to ROBERT BROWN. Q. Was it blood or Port wine? A. Having been brought up a butcher I think I am too good a judge of blood to be deceived; I should think I have not less than 50l. worth of wine and spirits: I think it possible there might be another engaged in it.
Prisoner's Defence. I never entered the place with the key in my pocket; I was not certain that it would have fitted it - it is not reasonable that I should have returned to the prosecutor's house if I had robbed him.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300415-301]
966. MARY WARD was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of April, 1 sheet, value 1s.; 2 pinafores, value 1s.; 1 bolster, value 4s. 5d.; 2 pairs of boots, value 12s.; 2 shirts, value 2s.; 1 pair of trousers, value 1s. 6d.; 4 handkerchiefs, value 2s.; 1 shawl, value 1s.; 1 frock, value 1s., and 1 cap, value 1s., the goods of Joshua Flight, her master.
JOSHUA FLIGHT. I am bar-man at a wine-vaults. The prisoner was servant to me for twelve months; she left me without notice, and I missed this property.
GEORGE LANCASTER. I am servant to a pawnbroker in Goswell-road. I have a number of these articles pawned by the prisoner.
JOSEPH JOHN GOODGE. I have three pinafores and a frock, pawned by the prisoner, I believe; she is in the habit of coming to the shop.
WILLIAM CRABB. I took the prisoner, and found the duplicates on her.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that the prosecutor engaged her as housekeeper, to take the charge of four children, but was too poor to supply them with the common necessaries or pay her wages; that she had pledged the articles to procure his children food.
JOSHUA FLIGHT re-examined. Q. Have you been in such a state of indigence that you have employed her to pawn your property, or allowed her to do it? A. No - I allowed her money as a housekeeper; she has pawned her own property - I do not owe her any thing; I passed my word at a tally-shop, and a man has come to me for it.
Four witnesses gave the prisoner a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 49.
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury.
Confined One Month.

970. THOMAS PHENY was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of February, 2 lbs. weight of soap, value 14d., the goods of Thomas Winmill; and that he had before been convicted of felony.
THOMAS WINMILL. I am a grocer, and live in Carnaby-street. I was out at the time this happened.
JOHN MATTHEWS. I live in Marlborough-court, near the prosecutor's; I saw the prisoner near his shop, with three other persons - the prisoner went and asked for one halfpenny worth of Spanish liquorice, while one of the others took the soap from the counter; the prisoner then took the soap - I gave an alarm, and he was taken in Maddox-street, with the soap, which he dropped.
WILLIAM DODD. I saw the prisoner put the soap under his coat, as I was standing in Lowndes-court; we went and asked at the prosecutor's shop, if they had lost any thing - they said Yes, a piece of soap; we pursued the prisoner and met an officer, who took him.
JOHN ANDERSON. I took the prisoner - this is the soap.
Prisoner's Defence. I was standing still when they took me; the soap was on before me. I went to buy the Spanish liquorice, and one of the boys took the soap; I did not. GUILTY.
EDWARD BURRIDGE. I have a certificate of the conviction of the prisoner on the 29th of October - I was present, and know he is the person.
GUILTY. Aged 16. - Transported for Life.

971. JAMES HOBSON was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of April, 4 cruets, value 18d., and 1 cruet-stand, value 6d.; the goods of Thomas Pickett Hemmings; and JOHN DAVIS was indicted for feloniously receiving, on the same day, the same goods, well-knowing them to have been stolen.
EDWARD FRY. I know the shop of Mr. Thomas Pickett Hemmings, in High-street, St. Giles'. About ten o'clock, in the evening, on the 6th of April, I saw Hobson take the cruet-frame and four bottles from a shelf in the shop, just inside; he ran away - I gave an alarm; I went to look after him, but could not see him; I did not know him before.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. This was ten o'clock at night? A. Yes, the gas was alight; I saw his face - he made off in a short time: I was close to him.
THOMAS HOBBS. On the 7th of April I was going on duty - I saw Davis with something under his coat, and Hobson by his side; I said, "What have you got there?" he said, "This, and this boy gave it to me" - they both said they were going to sell it; Hobson said another boy gave it to him - I took the other boy by his description, but he was acquitted by the Magistrate.
Cross-examined. Q. And he told you where he could be found? A. Yes, he said he had a shiny hat on, and I found him.
ELIZA HEMMINGS. I am the wife of the prosecutor. The witness came and told me of this; I pursued the boy, but could not find him.
HOBSON - GUILTY. Aged 12.
Confined Three Months.
DAVIS - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300415-307]
972. ELIZA HARRIS was indicted for that she, on the 5th of March, Ellen Bonsey, a child of Robert Bonsey and Lydia, his wife, under the age of ten years, to wit, of the age of two years, feloniously by force, did take and carry away, with intent to deprive the said Robert Bonsey and Lydia, his wife, of the possession of the said child, by concealing and detaining the said child from them; against the Statute, &c.
2nd COUNT, like the first, only substituting the words"by fraud," instead of force.
3rd COUNT, like the first, only with intent to steal certain articles of apparel about the person of the said child, to wit, 1 pinafore, value 3d.; 1 frock, value 4d.; 1 hat, value 6d.; 1 petticoat, value 2d.; 1 shift, value 2d., and 1 pair of boots, value 4d., the goods of the said Robert Bonsey.
4th COUNT, like the third, only stating it to be by fraud, instead of by force, &c.
ROBERT BONSEY. I am the husband of Lydia Bonsey - this little child is my daughter; she was two years old on the 25th of last January - her name is Ellen; we live in George-court, Strand. On the 5th of March I saw my child playing near the door as near seven o'clock as could be; I just wiped my fingers, ran out, and the child was gone - I ran out, and made inquiries till half-past four o'clock on the Sunday; this was on the Friday- I found her at the refuge for the houseless poor.
LYDIA BONSEY. I was out at the time, and when I came home I heard the child was gone - I made every inquiry, but did not hear of her till Sunday.
HENRY PARDOE. I am a Police-constable. On the 7th of March I saw the prisoner in Middle-row, St. Giles', at half-past one o'clock in the morning; she was sitting down with this child, and I said, "What do you do here, my good woman? why don't you go home?" she said, "I have no home;" I said, "I can take you to the refuge;" I took her there, and the superintendent would not take her in, as she had been there seven days, and was discharged for improper conduct; I had asked her if this was her child, and she said it was - I then asked her what she had done with the child while she was there; she said she had put it out at No. 22, Duck-lane, Westminster - I asked how she could pay for it; she said, by a few halfpence at a time - she had the child booked Tommy Harris; in taking her to the watch-house she said she had taken it from a door in the Strand, and I went to the place on the Monday, and found they had got the child.
GUILTY. Aged 23. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-308]
973. CHARLES THOMAS WILLIAMS was indicted for that he, on the 10th of April, feloniously did cut, break, and destroy 10 trees, that is to say, 4 cherrytrees, 4 plum-trees, and 2 apple-trees, value 10l., then standing and growing, the property of and belonging to Jane Flint, whereby the amount of injury done to the said Jane Flint, exceeded the sum of 5l., that is to say, amounted to the sum of 10l.; against the Statute, &c.
2nd COUNT, like the first, only substituting the word"damage" instead of destroy.
3rd COUNT, for feloniously breaking and destroying in part, &c., like the first count.
4th COUNT, like the third, only stating that he did cut, break, and damage, instead of destroy.
JOHN BARKER. I am son-in-law to Jane Flint - she is a widow; I superintend her garden; she is a market-gardener, at Isleworth. On Saturday evening,the 10th of April, I saw the trees in her garden all safe, between six, and seven o'clock, and the next morning, between five and six, I found four plum-trees and four cherry-trees had been broken down and destroyed; one pulled up, and the others destroyed - the branches were all off; they had been against a wall; I afterwards found two apple-trees were destroyed, which had been full of bloom - they had been all alive, and were worth more than 10l. - I saw some footmarks, and had suspicion of the prisoner, from some language which he had used to me on the Saturday night; Mrs. Flint had warned the prisoner off her ground, and I had told the foreman to warn him off - I keep the Phoenix public-house, and on the Saturday night the prisoner was there; he wanted to kick up a row with six or seven others, and I told him to go along; he came, put his fist in my face, and said,"D-n your eyes, you bl-y b-r, I will go down the garden, and destroy every bl-y tree you have got"- I therefore thought he was the person, I fetched the constable to the garden, and sent him to take him on suspicion; his right shoe was taken off, and fitted to the marks in the ground; they matched exactly - he was sent to the cage; we went on board the prisoner's barge, and found some brocoli, which fitted to the stumps left in the ground - they had tried to break into the house, and if they had they would have done much more mischief.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Were there not two persons taken? A. Yes; one is here as a witness - the nails fitted exactly in the footmarks; it was the right shoe - I do not know how many nails there were; there might be twenty-four - there were six nail marks in the ground; the prisoner had been drinking, but he was not so drunk but he knew what he was going to do - he was not sober.
JACOB CROWTHER. I am a constable. I went on board a barge, and found the prisoner, on Easter Sunday morning, about seven o'clock, with his companion, who is here; I took the prisoner into custody, he took off his shoe at the garden, and gave it me to fit to the impression on the ground - it fitted in the nail marks, and in the impression where there were no nails; it was fitted in the presence of half a dozen people - I found as much brocoli on board the barge as would tie up in a common sized handkerchief; I saw two of the heads fitted to the stumps, and they matched exactly.
CHARLES GARDNER. I know the prisoner, and was with him in a barge on the river, on the Saturday evening - he told me he was going to break all the bl-y trees that Barker had got in his place; I went with him to the garden, pulled up a few brocoli, he broke the trees, and then we went on board the barge - we were not more than half an hour in the garden; he pulled up some strawberry plants - I took the brocoli on board.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. I hope you were sober?" A. Yes - he was a little the worse for liquor; he did all the mischief, except taking up the brocoli - he made me go against my will to the garden, take up the brocoli, and take it on board the barge.
JOHN BARKER re-examined. Q. Did they pull up the trees by the roots? A. This one was broken down, and the root nearly out of the ground - the whole of the trees were destroyed.
GUILTY. Aged 23. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-309]
974. CATHERINE WOOD, ELIZABETH CARPENTER and MARY DILLON were indicted for stealing, on the 14th of April, 1 jacket, value 1s., and 1 pair of trousers, value 1s., the goods of Mary Bennett.
MARY BENNETT. I keep a clothes-shop, in Brook-street, Stepney. On the 14th of April, about six o'clock, Carpenter came in, and asked for a pair of shoes - she had a child in her arms; I had seen her a number of times -I said I had no shoes; she said I had, and then Wood, who was quite intoxicated, and another person came in -I did not see Dillon to my knowledge; I was afraid of their doing some mischief, and I wished to get Wood out - the third person said she would get her out; they went out - Carpenter remained; I put my hand to her and said, "Come again by and by;" none of them bought any thing - I closed my door as I was going out, and the next morning I missed a jacket and a pair of trousers, which the officer has.
EMANUEL HARRIS. I keep a clothes-shop. On the morning after this Carpenter and Dillon came to my house, both very drunk, with this jacket and trousers on their arms - I told them to go off, and they did; I have no doubt this is the jacket by the buttons.
JOHN DOUGLASS. I apprehended Wood at her lodgings, on the morning of the 15th of April - and found this boy's dress there.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Wood's Defence, Carpenter had my child to nurse -I was at Greenwich fair on Sunday, Monday and Wednesday; I know not how the dress came in my place, but when Carpenter had words with her husband she used to make my place her own.
Carpenter's Defence. I do not remember being at Mrs. Bennett's: I might have been there, but I never saw the dress till I saw it at this woman's - I was intoxicated; I suppose I went to buy the shoes, but I do not know.
JOHN DOUGLASS re-examined. Q. What distance do they live from each other? A. About fifty yards, in the same passage.
CARPENTER - GUILTY. Aged 39.
WOOD - NOT GUILTY.
DILLON - NOT GUILTY.
975. THE SAID ELIZABETH CARPENTER and MARY DILLON were indicted for stealing, on the 15th of April, 1 sheet, value 2s., the goods of Thomas Brown.
MARY BROWN. I am the wife of Thomas Brown; he lives in Ratcliff-highway. I sent my little niece to the pawnbroker with two sheets and one blanket; one of the sheets is quite lost - I afterwards went to the pawnbroker, who gave me a description of Carpenter and Dillon; I went to Mr. Harris and said, "I had been robbed" - he said, "Of a sheet, Mrs. Brown?" I said, "Yes, how came you to know it?" he said, "Two women have been here drunk, and wanted to sell it;" I then went down a court and saw Carpenter looking out at a window - I said to her, "Have you seen my little girl?" she said Yes - I said"Have you taken any thing out of her basket?" she said, No - she came down, and I walked with her some time; at last she said she had had the sheet of Dillon; we went to Dillon, who said she had not had a sheet for months - I said if she would give it up, I would say no more about it.
EMANUEL HARRIS. Carpenter and Dillon came to myhouse intoxicated - I would not let them in; Mrs. Brown came to me and said, "If you go down Brook-street, you will see a dark woman;" Dillon did not come into the shop.
JAMES MURPHY. I took Carpenter, and asked her about the sheet which she had offered to Harris - she said it belonged to Dillon; they were both sitting drinking tea - I asked where it was; she said she would not tell me - on the way to the station Carpenter said if we could make up the money, she would not hurt us.
Carpenter's Defence. I went to the pawnbroker's, and the child stood back; there were many people - I took the child up, put the things out, and laid them on the table; they would not take my article in - I walked away; I had not another thing to pawn - the woman came and said I had robbed her child, but I had not; there were a great many people there, and I left the articles on the counter; I never was in prison in my life before.
CARPENTER - GUILTY, Aged 39.
Transported for Seven Years for each offence.
DILLON - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300415-310]
976. ELIZABETH TAYLOR was indicted for stealing, on the 3rd of April, 1 sheet, value 10s., and 1 shawl, value 4s., the goods of Thomas Russell.
SARAH RUSSELL. I am the wife of Thomas Russell. The prisoner lodged at our house, in Westminster, for nine days - she had only part of a bed; it was not mentioned whether it was to be taken by the night or not - she went away without giving me notice, she owed something; when she had been gone six or seven days I missed a sheet and a silk shawl.
WALTER TANNANT. I am a pawnbroker, in the employ of Mr. Rochford. I have a sheet; I cannot say who pawned it.
ISAAC CUTHBERTSON. I took the prisoner, and found eleven duplicates on her, one for this sheet, and this shawl I found at her sister's, No. 20, Great Barlow-street.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 24. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-311]
977. WILLIAM TROTT and GEORGE JONES were indicted for stealing, on the 16th of April, 60 penny pieces, the monies of Henry Harben and William Larking.
JAMES THOMAS HORNSBY. I am in the service of Henry Harben and William Larking, cheesemonger s, Whitechapel. In the afternoon of the 16th of April, the two prisoners came into the shop together and had something, but I do not remember what - there was 5s. worth of penny-pieces tied up in a brown paper on the counter; both the prisoners went near to it, and Jones took it up and moved it towards Trott, who took it and put it into his pocket; they paid me a shilling - I then walked round and took Trott, who took the paper out of his pocket and threw it behind a tub of butter; I left him in the counting-house and ran and took the other prisoner.
- I saw Jones come out with a piece of cheese in his hand; I then saw him with Hornsby, and took charge of him - these are the pennypieces; I marked them.
TROTT - GUILTY. Aged 16.
JONES - GUILTY. Aged 12.
Confined Three Months.

978. JOSEPH RAINES was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of March, 1 jacket, value 12s.; 1 waistcoat, value 7s.; 1 pair of trousers, value 10s.; 1 shirt, value 3s., and 1 handkerchief, value 1s., the goods of William Kating, in a certain vessel on the navigable river Thames.

979. PHILIP PHILLIPS and WILLIAM PHILLIPS were indicted for stealing, on the 5 th of April, 2 window sashes, value 1l. 10s., the goods of Robert Warton, being fixed to a certain building.
JOHN POWELL. I am an officer. I went to the Crooked Billet, at the corner of King David-lane; I asked Philip Phillips whether the sashes he had there were his own - he said Yes; that he had received them from his master, pointto his brother William: I asked William where he got them - he said from his master in Fore-street, Limehouse; I said I should wish to see his master, and we went out to go there - in going along, I asked Philip Phillips if William had given them to him to carry; William said,"Carry be d-d, one is as bad as the other:" I took them to the station-house, and next day found a house with three pair of sashes gone - this pair fitted exactly.
ROBERT WARTON. Powell showed me a pair of sashes, which I had lost with two other pairs, from a house of mine in Dorset-street, Commercial-road - this pair fitted exactly; I had seen them about a month before - the prisoner had worked for me as carpenter, at that very house.
ANN WILLIS. I saw a man going over the wall of this house, ten minutes before eleven o'clock; I asked where he was going - he said to repair the house, and I saw no more of him.
MR. WARTON. There was a wall six or seven feet high to get over; the sashes had been fixed.
P. PHILLIPS - GUILTY. Aged 30.
W. PHILLIPS - GUILTY. Aged 24.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18300415-314]
980. MARY MURPHY was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of April, 1 jacket, value 10s., the goods of Charles Gray.
CHARLES GRAY. I am a mariner, and was paid off; I left Portsmouth on Tuesday night, and got to town on Wednesday, the 14th of April. I met the prisoner and went home to sleep with her; I gave her 2s. 6d. for the bed - I was not quite sober; but I knew what I was about - she said, "Lie down my dear, and let me pull your jacket off;" I pull it off and put it under my head - I went to sleep and when I awoke the house was open, and my jacket gone; I went out to look for her, and where should I find her but in New Gravel-lane, or somewhere there about; I do not know whether there was any money in my jacket pocket, but I had treated her very handsome, and the landlady said I had; I gave 9d. towards the child's frock; when I saw the prisoner I said, "You are the person" - I caught hold of her; and said, "Go for an officer:""Come along (said she) and we will have half a pint" - we went on to the Duke of York; but I would not go in; five or six girls came round and snatched her from me - I did not see her any more that night, but I told the officer.
A. E. REYNOLDS. I live in High-street, Shadwell. Onthe 14th of April the prisoner brought this jacket to pawn; I asked if it was her own - she said No, she was sent by John Gray; - I took it of her.(Property produced and sworn to.)
CHARLES GRAY re-examined. Q. How came she to know your name? A. It was on my trousers, we are forced to have it on board a man of war.
Prisoner's Defence. He came to the house where I was with another female; they went up stairs - he came down in half an hour, and said he had no more money; would I go and take his jacket - this is the truth, he said he would send to his landlady to come and redeem it; I asked his name, and he said Gray.
GEORGE PHILLIPS. I met the prosecutor between ten and eleven o'clock on Wednesday night; he was not drunk - he said this woman had robbed him: I said at that time of night I could not find her - but he had better come to me next morning; he came - I went to several places, and could not find her: I took the landlady of the house, and said if I could not find the prisoner, I should detain her; she used every exertion, and we found the prisoner in the Match-walk - I went to Mr. Reynold's; and they said they had no such jacket - I went again and found it there.
GUILTY. Aged 28. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-315]
Fourth Middlesex Jury. - Before Mr. Common Sergeant.
981. FREDERICK BROOKS was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of April, 1 blanket, value 9d.; 1 quilt, value 1s.; 2 pillow-cases, value 1s.; and 1 sheet, value 2s., the goods of Samuel Denby.
SARAH DENBY. I am the wife of Samuel Denby. The prisoner had a furnished room in our house - I missed the articles stated, and had him taken on the 15th of April; I had let these articles to the prisoner.
JOSEPH BEAL. I am an officer. I was sent for and took the prisoner - I found four duplicates of the articles, which were pawned; they were in a drawer, and he gave them to me.
HENRY ROBERT REDWOOD. I am in the service of a pawnbroker - I gave these two duplicates to a person who pawned this blanket and pillow; these other two were written by a young man in our service.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. They would have been redeemed on the Saturday following - I had a job, and wanted 5s. worth of varnish.
GUILTY. Aged 27. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-316]
982. CHARLES DUNN and THOMAS WOODROFFE were indicted for stealing, on the 1st of March, 1 sack, value 2s., and 280 lbs. weight of flour, value 48s., the goods of Edward Westrup.
EDWARD WESTRUP. I am a miller, and live at Bromley, in Middlesex. The prisoners were in my employ on the 1st of March; Dunn was a carter, and Woodroffe a miller; Mr. Kingsford went to the mill on the 1st of March, and said something - I sent for Dunn the next day, and asked how he came by a sack of flour which he had sold to Mr. Kingsford; he said he had not sold any, but afterwards he said, to tell the truth, he had it of a baker in Gray's Inn-lane - we immediately sent to Mr. Perry, who returned an answer with which we were satisfied; Dunn was gone then, but his wife sent him over to us on the Wednesday - I think I had some conversation with him, but did not threaten or promise him any thing; nor did Shelswell in my hearing.
Cross-examined by MR. PRENDERGAST. Q. His wife sent him to you - do you not remember your father saying, in your presence, that if he would tell all the particulars nothing would be done to him? A. He did not in my presence - I was present when his wife said she would send him; nothing had passed between me and his wife - I was present when his wife said she would send him; nothing had passed between me and his wife - I do not know what induced her to send him, except she thought we should get him; my father had some conversation with his wife - I did not tell Dunn that if he would tell all the truth I would pardon him.
Q. Now you see his wife is here - did not your father tell her it would be better if he would come? A. Not in my hearing - my father officiates there, but the business is only mine.
COURT. Q. Did your father say to Dunn's wife, "If he comes and tells the truth he shall not suffer for it?" A. Not to my knowledge; I did not hear any thing of that kind - when Dunn came he said he had sold a sack to Kingsford for 48s., and that Woodroffe had 15s. of it; Meredith, another carter, had received part of it - he said he had sold various others to the amount of eight sacks altogether; he said he had given Perry a sack of flour to sell - I left Dunn in custody; Shelswell brought Woodroffe.
THOMAS SHELSWELL. I am an officer. I was present when the prisoners were taken - I told Woodroffe I took him for being connected with others in robbing his employer of some flour; he said if I had a mind to hear what he had to say, he would tell the truth - I said he might do as he chose; he said he was coming to work and met Dunn, who gave him 15s., and told him that was his share of a sack of flour which had been sold, but he was not to say any thing about it; he considered it was a sack belonging to his master.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. What flour it was, was not specified? A. No - I did not know who his employer was.
JOHN KINGSFORD. On the 1st of March Dunn called on me with a sack of flour, and said he had been to Mr. Orton with five sacks, and he had not money enough to pay for more than four, and would I take the other; I said how much was it - he said 50s.; I said I would give him 47s. - he said he should lose 3s. by that; I gave him 48s.
THOMAS PRENDERGRASS. I was with Shelswell when Dunn was taken - he began talking a great deal about selling one sack here, and another sack there; I said he could expect no mercy, I must take him before the Magistrate - he said he wished to act like an honest man, and that he had taken eight sacks in the whole.
Dunn received a good character.
DUNN - GUILTY. Aged 24.
Confined Three Months.
WOODROFFE - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300415-317]
983. CHARLES HURNDELL was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of March, 1 waistcoat, value 2s.; 1handkerchief, value 6d., and 1 comb, value 2d., the goods of John McLachlan.
CHARLES HIPKINS. I am an officer. I saw the prosesecutor on the 25th of March, who said he had lost his clothes - I went to Brunswick-place, Commercial-road, with him; he asked if the prisoner was at home - his mother said No; he asked to see his bag, and these things were turned out of it - I went in search of the boy, and found him; I asked what he had done with the waistcoat, if he had pawned it - he said No - I took him to the station, and asked him again - then he said he had pawned it for 1s.; I went to the pawnbroker's, and found it, with this half handkerchief, which the prosecutor said had been in one when he had it - the prisoner then said he took it from the ship; the prosecutor is now gone to sea, but he saw the property and swore to it.
CHARLES WILLIAMSON. I am a pawnbroker. On the 25th of March the prisoner pawned this waistcoat with me; the prosecutor saw it, and swore to it.
Prisoner's Defence. The comb that was found in my bag I had had three or four months - I bought it myself; the sailor said it was his.
GUILTY. Aged 14.
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury.
Fined 1s. and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18300415-318]
984. EDWARD HAMLIN was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of March, 1 till, value 1s., and 4 shillings, the property of James Greig.
SARAH SUTLE. I am servant to James Greig; he is a baker. I was sitting in the parlour behind the shop, at half-past seven o'clock in the evening, and heard money chinking - I got up, and saw a boy run out with the till in his hand; I ran to the door, and called Stop thief! he set the till down four or five doors off, and ran away - the next witness brought him back; I had not seen his face -I saw no other boy; I saw him when the witness pursued him; the till was taken up, and brought to me - we could not tell whether any thing was missing from it.
WILLIAM MATTHEWS. I was standing opposite the prosecutor's and saw the prisoner run from the shop with the till; he set it down four doors off - I took him about eight doors further, without losing sight of him.
GUILTY. Aged 11. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-319]
985. JOHN BRANNOR was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of March. 1 lb. weight of sole leather, value 2s., the goods of John Giblett.
JOHN GIBLETT. On the 18th of March I was out; my man gave me some information - I did not miss any leather, but here is some which I know to be mine.
JACKSON GIBLETT. On the 18th of March the prisoner came to the shop - I saw him put part of this leather into a bag, which he laid on the counter; he then went on buying some other leather, which he paid for - he was going away; I said he had more leather, which he had not paid for - I put my hand on the bag, and these four pieces of leather were in it.
Prisoner. I went with a friend to buy two pairs of soles; he selected a pair, and paid for them - I said, "If you will pay for the three pairs, I will settle with you when the boots I have to sole for you are done." Witness. There was one pair paid for.
EDMUND NEWMAN. I am a shopman. The prisoner came in with another person, and said I was to look out a pair of 1s. 2d. soles for this man; I showed him some at 1s. 2d., and 1s. 3d. - he said, "I don't think these are quite good enough," and while I turned to get a pair at 1s. 4d., he put these two pairs in his bag - Jackson Giblett came round, and asked if I knew these men; I said No - the other man paid 1s., and the prisoner paid 4d.; they were then going out, Giblett put his hand on the bag, and these were found in it - he said nothing at first, but afterwards said it was a mistake.
Prisoner's Defence My friend was to pay for them, and I said, "Have you paid for the whole?" he said, Yes; and I thought he had.
EDMUND NEWMAN. They had one pair of soles, and the other man and the prisoner paid for them; they stood side by side, and could hear what was said - I think the prisoner was in liquor.
MR. GIBLETT. I think he was rather in liquor.
GUILTY. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18300415-320]
986. WILLIAM COLE was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of February, 15 silver thimbles, value 15s., the goods of Rowland Hastings, his master.
ROWLAND HASTINGS. I am a silver thimble-maker, and live in Great Sutton-street; the prisoner was in my employ for thirteen years. On the 26th of February we watched the prisoner, and he was seen to take these thimbles - we had missed some the day before.
CHARLES CAMERON. I am an officer. I was sent for on the 26th of February; I took the prisoner, and found in his fob pocket eleven thimbles, and a duplicate relating to four others - the prisoner said he meant to take them back again.
LEONARD CLARE MATTHEWS. I am a pawnbroker. I took in these thimbles on the 26th of February, of a person I cannot swear to, but I believe it was the prisoner.
JOSEPH AUBERRY. I am in the prosecutor's employ. I saw the prisoner take the thimbles out of the bowls, and put them into his right hand pocket.
SAMUEL VESTY. I am in the prosecutor's employ. We were all ordered into the counting-house to be searched, and these were found on the prisoner.
Prisoner's Defence. I earned 30s. of my master, and he stopped 15s. 6d. out of it - my wife and child were turned out of doors; my child is blind, and is an idiot.
ROWLAND HASTINGS. I had had the prisoner from a boy- he at first had 5s. a week, and went on till he had 24s. a week; if I had been going out on a Sunday, I should have asked him to come and mind my house; he is an object of great pity.
GUILTY. Aged 29.
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury. - Judgement Respited .
