989. MARY ANN JONES was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of March, 1 watch, value 1l.; 1 seal, value 3s.; 2 watch-keys, value 2s.; 1 ring, value 2d., and 1 watch-ribbon, value 1d., the goods of Thomas Walker, from his person.
JAMES DITTUM. I was going to my work at half-past one o'clock in the morning, on the 13th of March, and met the prisoner - the prosecutor was following her, crying Stop thief! and she ran towards me; I went to stop her, she turned back; I ran and took her - she fell down, and threw a watch from her; I held her down till the prosecutor came up, and took her - I saw the constable take up the watch, and I took up the case; the prosecutor charged her with stealing it, in my presence - she said it was not her, it was another girl with her.
WILLIAM EVERETT. I am an officer. I heard Stop thief! called, but did not see the prisoner taken; on going down Gold's-hill I saw the prosecutor, who said she had got his watch from him; she said she had not taken it, but another girl had - she ran, and was stopped; I asked where the other girl threw the watch - she pointed out the place; I took it up, and the witness took the case - the prosecutor gave his name as Thomas Walker - he is master of a vessel.
Prisoner. Q. Did I not say I was not the person? A. Yes; I saw the prosecutor and her stand at the end of Gold's-hill. as I was going on my beat, five minutes before the alarm; there was another girl at the other corner - the prosecutor said it was his watch; he is gone to sea.
Prisoner's Defence. I met the man, who asked me to go home with him; I said I would not - I saw him talking to another woman; I went down Three Cups-alley - he came and took hold of me, and I said, "Let me go - I will walk with you;" we went on to Gold's-hill, and this witness came, and took me.
GUILTY (of stealing, but not from the person.) Aged 16.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-324]
990. JOHN MASON was indicted for embezzlement.
THOMAS EVANS. I am a milkman. The prisoner was in my employ, and was to receive money, which he was to pay as soon as he came home.
FRANCIS DAVISON. I paid the prisoner 11d. on the 14th of March, about eight o'clock in the morning, for his master.
ELIZABETH FOSTER. I am in Evans' employ. I never received the 11d. - he says he put it on the table; my master was very sleepy, and we think he took it up.
ELIZABETH DAVIS. I was in the prosecutor's employ. The prisoner never brought his account right; he never paid me the 11d.
JOHN EDDS. I met the prisoner on the 16th; I took him up for a waistcoat which he had on; the prosecutor swore to that at first, but denied it afterwards.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300415-325]
991. GEORGE HOWSE was indicted for embezzlement.
JOHN MARRIOTT. I am a carman. The prisoner was in my employ on the 25th of February; he sometimes received money for me, and was to bring it me on that day - he received 5s. from Mr. Gray, which he never paid me; he did not return.
GEORGE GRAY. I paid the prisoner 5s. on the 25th of February, to take to Mr. Marriott, for him to buy a sack of potatoes.
Prisoner. He said I was to bring them - he did not say I was to give it to Mr. Marriott. Witness. I meant it for Mr. Marriott; he had his horse and cart with him.
THOMAS FARRANT. I am an officer. I took the prisoner on the 26th of February; I charged him with stealing this - he said he was very sorry, but he had the misfortune to lose it.
GUILTY. Aged 25. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18300415-326]
992. THOMAS HAY was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of April, 1 watch, value 30s.; 1 watch chain, value 3s., and 2 seals, value 3s., the goods of James Dingwell, from his person.
JAMES DINGWELL. I am a carpenter, and live in Edward-street, Battle-bridge. On the 13th of April I was drinking at the Maidenhead public-house - I came out for a short time, and was sick; I sat down, and saw the prisoner there - he offered me help, and advised me to give him my watch to take into the bar, or perhaps I should be robbed of it; I did not give it him - he unbuttoned my waistcoat, and said by getting the air I should be relieved: he then unbuttoned the band of my trousers: I looked down, and saw my watch hanging, (I believe.) by a button; I grasped at it, but he was too active - he got it, and handed it to another person, who got off; I seized the prisoner, took him into the house, and charged him with stealing it - he opened his waistcoat, and pleaded innocence; he said he was not the man - he then attempted to run away, but he did not escape my notice; I saw an officer, and gave him in charge.
Cross-examined by MR. HEATON. Q. When was this? A. On Tuesday afternoon - I am sure it was not Monday; I believe I had drank a small glass of gin at another public-house in the morning - I might have some porter at eleven o'clock; we had some ale at the Maidenhead - this happened between four and five o'clock; the ale had not impaired my intellects - I went into a corner which was parted off; I did not sit down in the fifth, there is a stone on which I sat - I was almost asleep when he aroused me; I looked at my watch after he came up - I was not in such a state that I did not know whether I had my watch; he came to me two or three times - I was not asleep, I was heavy.
WILLIAM FOSTER. I saw the prosecutor on the 13th of April, against the Maidenhead - I saw the prisoner take something out of his pocket and give it to another, but what I cannot say; the other ran away - the prosecutor seized the prisoner, and gave him in charge.
Cross-examined. Q. What did you do? A. Nothing -I did not go after the young man who ran off; he turned too quick for me - I did not see the prisoner put his hand into the prosecutor's pocket, but I saw it against the band of his breeches; I think he was unbuttoning it.
SAMUEL PRIME. I know the prosecutor - he lodged with me; I saw his watch about two o'clock that afternoon.
THOMAS WOFFINDEN. I am a Police-officer. I was going round Battle-bridge - the prosecutor had the prisoner by the collar, and charged him with taking his watch; he said he did not take it.
Cross-examined. Q. Was not the prosecutor beastlydrunk? A. No, sir; I believe he was capable of knowing what he was about - he appeared to me to be sensible.
Prisoner's Defence. I am innocent.
JAMES PARKER. On the 13th of April I was at this public-house - I went for a pot of porter about four o'clock in the afternoon; I saw the prosecutor there very drunk, and several persons were round him - I saw him staggering and stupified; I looked stedfastly at him and did not see any watch in his possession; I think I should have seen it if he had one.
HENRY DAVIES. On Easter Tuesday I was playing with the prisoner about four o'clock - the prosecutor was outside the door of the Maidenhead, sitting on the flag stone, in the fifth, very drunk indeed; there was no other boy with us - the prosecutor caught hold of him, when we were about half a dozen yards from him.
COURT. Q. What did he say? A. He said, "You have got my watch;" he said, "I have not, I have not seen it;" I had not seen any one with the prisoner.
ELIZA HAY. I am the prisoner's sister, and am going on for thirteen years of age - I was going to my mother when this gentleman caught hold of my brother, and said, "You have got my watch;" my brother said, "I have not, nor have I seen it;" I had seen my brother before he caught hold of him - he was standing by himself, with his hands in his pockets, and when the prosecutor was going along the boys hooted him, he was so very tipsy.
COURT. Q. Where did the gentleman come from? A. Out of the house, and seized my brother.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 20. - Confined Three Months.

Second Middlesex Jury - Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.

994. JOSEPH WREN was indicted for that he, on the 17th of March, a certain building within the curtilege of the dwelling-house of George Tickner, feloniously did break and enter, and steal therein, 1 live tame goose, price 7s.; 1 live tame gander, price 7s.; 1 live tame duck, price 3s.; 11 live tame fowls, price 30s., and 20 eggs, value 1s., his property.
GEORGE TICKNER. My dwelling-house is the Custom-house at the West India-docks. I keep poultry in a large out-house, which is enclosed with my garden and dwelling-house, by a wall seven or eight feet high. On Tuesday evening, the 16th of March, about eight o'clock, the fowls were all locked up safe: I went to feed them next morning, and found the tiles taken off, and part of the grape-vine which goes over it down the windowframes were broken, and on the wall were marks of the prisoner's toes having scraped down; they had got in by breaking the window-frames out - I missed all my fowls, two geese, one duck, and twenty-four eggs, which I had marked with a cross in ink on each side.
JAMES LEE. I am an officer. On Wednesday, the 17th of March, the prosecutor informed me of this robbery, and about four o'clock that afternoon I was going to a house on Bow-common, and met the prisoner with two men named Wilson and McEwen on the side of the Regent's-canal, about half a mile from the prosecutor's; one of the men, not in custody, was carrying a bag across his shoulder: I stopped him, and asked him what he had got - he said he did not know; at that time the prisoner and Wilson walked on - I asked the man how he got it: he said he was employed by one of those men who had walked on; I told Mr. Prendergast to go after them - he went, and hearing somebody behind, they immediately took to their heels and ran - I caught hold of the man who had the bag; we struggled; he knocked me down and got away - I took the bag, opened it, and in it were seven hens, a cock just killed (it was quite warm), a duck, and two live geese: I proceeded on to the house on Bowcommon, having been informed that Wilson lived there - I found a great quantity of property there; I had been there about fifteen minutes, and while I was in the kitchen the prisoner and Wilson came in, and opened the door; seeing me in the kitchen, they immediately ran out of the house - I pursued them over a bridge, and they got away; in the house I found two of the prosecutor's hens, sitting on twenty-four eggs, which he identified - they were marked with a cross; next morning about six o'clock I apprehended the prisoner in bed, at a lodging-house at Stratford, and asked how he came to run away with Wilson the day before - he denied it, and said he had not seen Wilson for a month; he was taken to the office, and stated to the Magistrate that he had seen Wilson.
GRIFFIN. I work at the East India-dock warehouse, and live next door to the prosecutor. About two o'clock in the morning I heard a noise like fowls and geese going in; I had heard some persons go out, but could not see them - I heard a noise two nights.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. When I found what they had in the bag I came away.
GUILTY. - Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18300415-329]
Before Mr. Justice Park.
995. THOMAS GAMBLE was indicted for attempting to administer and causing to be taken by John Thompson, a certain poison, (i.e.) oil of vitrol, with intent of his malice aforethought to kill and murder him.
SECOND COUNT, calling it a certain destructive thing instead of poison.
JOHN MAXSEY. I am thirteen years old. I was at the factory where the prisoner worked with Thompson on the 15th of February; I gave this stuff to Thompson - I do not know what it was; it was a powder - the prisoner sent me to the doctor's for it, and told me to ask for jalap; I paid 1d. for it - it was jalap.
The prosecutor was dumb; his mother undertook to communicate with him by signs, but after the above evidence it was unnecessary.
NOT GUILTY.

998. SHADRACH MITCHELL and JOSEPH WILLIAMS were indicted for feloniously assaulting William Stevens, on the King's highway, on the 29th of March, putting him in fear, and taking from his person, and against his will, 1 handkerchief, value 9d., and 7 shillings, his property.
WILLIAM STEVENS. I am a farmer's man, and live at Long-ford. On the 29th of March I came to Hounslow, between four and five o'clock in the afternoon, with a friend; we went to the White Bear, and had three pints of beer between us and another man, who lived in the yard - we then went to the Prince Regent, staid there about half an hour, and two of us drank three pints of beer - I walked about the town, and then went to the Cricketers, on the heath, alone, and had a pint of ale; I got there about halfpast six o'clock, and staid about an hour - the prisoners came in while I was there; I knew them before, and we had a pot of beer together - I got up to come home; they followed me out - Williams caught hold of my arm, and said, "Let us go up to the George the Fourth, and have a pot;" I refused - Mitchell caught hold of my arm, and told me to come along; I refused - they pulled me about one hundred yards: Mitchell then threw me down, and held me, and then they took my handkerchief off my neck - they took nothing else; they ran away, and I after them- I met Dinam, and went with him for Bennett, an officer, who took them in the town, and my handkerchief was found in Williams' pocket - I cannot say which of them took it; I felt Williams take 7s. out of my left-hand pocket - that was not found; I had no purse - I had taken all my money out at the Cricketers, to pay for the beer; they sat at the same table, and could see it - I was not at all intoxicated.
Prisoner Williams. Q. When I came in did you not challenge me to toss for a pot? A. Yes, and I did; I said I had only a few halfpence in my pocket - I did that for a joke.
CHARLES BENNETT. I am a constable. Stevens came to my house soon after eight o'clock, and complained of having been robbed of 7s. and his handkerchief; I went in search of the prisoners - he told me who they were, but did not go with me; I found Williams against the cage, and apprehended him - I found this handkerchief in his breast pocket, under his smock-frock; he told me Mitchell was one that was with him - I went in search of him; a boy came and told me he was talking to Williams in the cage - I went and found him there, but found nothing on him; Mitchell said the handkerchief was his own - I never heard the prosecutor say it was not his; he was a little elevated, but not drunk.
Prisoner Mitchell. Q. When I was taken, did he not say he could not swear to me? A. I do not recollect it - he described your dress, and said he could swear to your dress.
WILLIAM STEVENS. This is the handkerchief I had round my neck; I know it by these holes in the middle -I have had it half a year.
JURY. Q. How came you to describe them by their dress? A. I said I could not swear to their names, but their dress; I have known them both nine or ten years, but had not seen them lately.
Mitchell's Defence. I did not lay him down, or take the handkerchief; I followed Williams and him out of the Cricketers, and saw them all going towards the Wellington.
Williams' Defence. I did not lay him down, nor put my hand into his pocket.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300415-333]
Before Mr. Justice Pask.
999. JOHN LASHAM, THOMAS MORGAN and WILLIAM SAGE were indicted for breaking and entering the dwelling-house of John Gould, on the 13th of April, and stealing 50 pairs of shoes, value 10l., his property: and WILLIAM LASHAM was indicted for feloniously receiving the same, knowing them to have been stolen.
JOHN GOULD. I live in Leader-street, Chelsea, and deal in slops. On the 11th of April I went to bed about eleven o'clock - I was the last person up, and fastened the doors and windows; I have three outside doors - on Monday morning, a few minutes before five o'clock, the milkman called me; I found my shop shutter cut open, and the glass broken - the shutter appeared to have been cut with a knife or saw: there was a hole about eight inches square - here is the piece that was cut out; a place had been chalked underneath - I missed between forty and fifty pairs of men's new shoes, which exceed 10l. in value.
STEPHEN BROWN. I am a watchman - my beat is at the top of Keppel-street, about twenty yards from the prosecutor's house. On Sunday night, the 11th of April, just as I had called twelve o'clock, I saw John Lasham, Morgan, Sage, and another not in custody, altogether, having some coffee at the stall in the road, where my wife sells coffee to carters, or any body - I knew the prisoners before.
CHARLES HAYWARD HUGHES. I am a constable of Chelsea. On Monday, the 12th of April, I went to William Lasham's house, No. 30, George-street, Chelsea, between seven and eight o'clock in the morning; I did not see him at home - I went again at night, and saw him - I assisted in searching the premises after taking Lasham, and found a fiddle answering the description in an advertisement; I went again in the morning, and in a dark closet under the stairs leading to his room, I found a basket full of men's new shoes - he sleeps in a room at the end of that passage; I had taken him into custody the night before, and on searching him at the watch-house he took something from the lining of his waistcoat, and dropped it behind him; I took it up - it was two duplicates of men's shoes, one pair of which Mr.Gould swears to - before I took him into custody, as I went up stairs I heard him and his wife quarrelling; she said to him, "Did not you sharpen two knives yesterday to cut Mr. Gould's shutter open?" and in the further progress of the quarrel I heard her say, "If you strike me I will call Murder!" then she said, "What business had you to go and get drunk with the money produced by the stealing of Mr. Gould's shoes?" he said, "You d - d old wh - e, you had as much of the money as I had;" she said, "Mr. Hughes and Brown are coming for you, Mr. Lasham - you will be locked up, Mr. Lasham; you will be hung, Mr. Lasham! Mr. Hughes has got the fiddle;" we then went into the room - Lasham was sitting there naked; I said, "Mr. Hughes is come. Mr. Lasham; do you know any thing about the fiddle I got here to-day?" I then asked if he knew any thing about the shoes - he said No; I asked for his clothes, and found 3s. in his pocket, and 2s. 6d. on his wife - I took Morgan, (who was in custody.) to him at the watch-house, and said to him, "I have been told that you said this morning that Morgan brought the shoes to your house" - he said, "Yes, Morgan brought them;" I took young Lasham into custody first - he denied the charge; he said he had been at the coffee-shop at Knightsbridge on Sunday night with Morgan Sage and young Lee; I asked what time they came back - he said about twelve o'clock; that they had some coffee of Brown, the watchman, and afterwards spent the night under a butcher's pent-house till another watchman drove them away - Morgan was brought to me in custody; I said, "Where did you spend your pay last night?" he said he had been to Knightsbridge; I asked who with - he said nobody; that he came back at half-past ten o'clock, and slept on Mr. Leach's stones all night - they are in Manor-street, near old Lasham's house; I said that was a lie, and told him he had been with the others - he denied it; I found Sage at a house of ill-fame, in Westminster, on Friday.
HENRY GARROD. I am shopman to a pawnbroker, in Bridge-street, Lambeth. I have a pair of shoes pawned by William Lasham for 2s. 6d., in the name of William West. No. 44, New-cut - the duplicate produced is what I gave him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300415-334]
Third London Jury. - Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
1000. ROBERT WALL was indicted for feloniously receiving, on the 21st of February, of a certain evil disposed person, 1 skeleton clock, value 19l.; 5 other clocks, value 53l.; 4 time pieces, value 30l.; 1 barometer, value 25s.; 1 watch, value 30s., and 2 sacks, value 3s., the goods of Isaac Leplastrier and another, he well-knowing them to have been stolen.
MR. ISAAC LEPLASTRIER. I am in partner ship with my son; this property was at our shop in Cheapside, at the corner of Old' Change - I and my son left the shop together about nine o'clock on Saturday night the 20th of February, the door was shut immediately - I have a porter constantly living and sleeping in a room adjoining the shop; the property was all safe then - I received a message from the porter between ten and eleven o'clock on Sunday night; I went to the shop and found it broken open, and property amounting to above 100l. taken, consisting of clocks of different descriptions, none of which has been recovered; the shop had been entered by means of a side door in the passage - they had got in at No. 49, Old'Change; picked the look in a passage, and got round into our shop: my shop is No. 11, Cheapside, and No. 49, Old'Change.
ANN PETTERIN FLOCKTON. On the Sunday in question between two and three o'clock in the afternoon, I saw some men come up Old'Change, arm-in-arm one was dressed in a light drab coat, he was a stiff upright man: the other was dressed in a blue coat - I saw the one in a light drab coat take a key out of his right hand pocket, and saw them open the door of No. 49, Old 'Change; they both walked in together into the house - I was called by the servant at a stationer's shop opposite, and might be there twenty minutes or a quarter of an hour; and when I came out, I saw a backney-coach standing at the door of No. 49, the coach was No. 44; I cannot read, but know figures very well - there were two 4's on the coach, the coachman held the door open; I saw the man in a blue coat that went in come out, and jump into the coach - then the man in the light coat, brought out a large blue bag as full as it could hold, it was more than he could lift; they put it into the coach; the coachman was at the door all the time - the man who brought out the bag locked the door; put the key into the same pocket as he took it out of, and then got into the coach, the coachman shut the door; they came right up Old 'Change, and turned round by St. Paul's; I saw the coachman as plain as possible - and swear positively that the prisoner is that man - he drove towards me, and no other coaches were coming by; the place was quite clear.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. What are you? A. A poor woman - I sweep the street; I mentioned this the day after the robbery, and gave the number of the coach; I do not make a practice of looking at the numbers of coaches - it was a wet day; I do not remember the number of any other coach I saw that day - I was standing on the opposite side - the coach came right facing me and turned round; I saw the prisoner holding the coach door - I saw the number when the coach turned round; it did not go very quick till it turned the corner - the middle part of the number shone like glass - it was a coach; (looking at two figures) that was not the number - that is 27; it did not rain very hard - I saw nobody else near the coach.
COURT. Q. There are not many coaches about on Sundays in that part of the town? A. The stand is just by - there is hardly any traffic on Sundays.
ELIZABETH HOSKINGS. I am a servant. On Sunday, the 21st of February, I lived at No. 48, Old 'Change, and saw a hackney-coach draw up to No. 49, about three o'clock - the prisoner is the coachman; I am quite certain.
Cross-examined. Q. What coloured coat had he? A. I do not know - his hat was covered with oil skin; I had never seen him before.
FRANCIS MORRIS. I am the prosecutor's porter, and slept there on Saturday, the 20th of February - I went to master's house on Sunday morning, at ten o'clock; returned about half-past eleven at night, and on enteringthe door in Cheapside by the key, I found a great deal of property taken - I had locked every thing up secure.
JEREMIAH LEACH. I was horse-keeper to Mr. Welch, a hackney-coach master in Horseferry-road - he has seven pairs of plates; No. 44 is one pair. On Sunday, the 21st of February, the prisoner drove No. 44 - I remember his going out about half-past seven o'clock in the morning with the coach, but I cannot say whether he drove it; he took it out, and had driven it for three or four days - he ought to have returned at five o'clock in the morning with the coach, but I cannot say whether he drove it; he took it out, and had driven it for three or four days - he ought to have returned at five o'clock in the evening; I cannot say at what time he came, as I was not in the yard.
Cross-examined. Q. He would come home at all hours - just as his fare required? A. Yes; I have known him five or six months - he is honest as far as I know; he wore a shiney hat - it was not oil skin, but looked like it.
DANIEL FORRESTER. I am an officer. I heard of this robbery on Monday morning, the 22nd of February - I went to Horseferry-road, and finding the prisoner drove No. 44 I went to his house, which is about fifty yards from Walsh's, in Horseferry-road, but did not see him; on Monday, the 1st of March, between eight and nine o'clock in the evening, I saw him going into the Mansion-house gate - I had gone frequently to where I was directed, but could not find him.
Cross-examined. Q. Was he not going into the Mansion-house to surrender himself? A. That I cannot answer - he was going in with Herdsfield.
CHARLES HERDSFIELD. I am an officer. The prisoner wrote a letter to my brother to meet him - I went with my brother, and met him by appointment and took him; he surrendered to us.
Prisoner's Defence. I surrendered - the female witness says she was in the house a quarter of an hour, and was in Cheapside when she saw me drive the coach - I do not recollect having been there at all; I am called on at a non plus, or else I could prove to the best of my knowledge I was in the Waterloo-road, and saw some fruitwomen taken to the watch-house for exposing fruit for sale - here is the hat I wore that day, and I had a boxcoat with six capes.
GUILTY. Aged 40. - Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-335]
001. JOHN HAYS was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of March, 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of Hugh Leslie, from his person.
CHARLES HERDSFIELD. On the 29th of March about one o'clock, I followed the prisoner from the Mansion-house, and saw him put his hand into the pocket of a gentleman who turned into the Bank; the prosecutor then came by, and he took his handkerchief - an apple-woman called out; he then threw it behind him - I laid hold of him, and called the prosecutor.
HUGH LESLIE. I was near the Bank; I felt nothing at my pocket - an apple-woman called me; I missed my handkerchief - I found it on the pavement; I gave the prisoner into custody.
Prisoner's Defence. Two boy s were walking behind the gentleman; one of them took the handkerchief and threw it behind - I caught it in my hand, and threw it on the ground; another lad was taken and was in Newgate, his friends have been drinking with the officer till two o'clock in the morning, to make it up.
CHARLES HERDSFIELD. I saw him take the handkerchief; another boy was taken with him, but the bill was thrown out.
GUILTY. Aged 15. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-336]
1002. LEWIS HENRY was indicted for stealing, on the 3rd of March, 1 gun, value 2l. 15s., the goods of Richard Groom.
RICHARD CHARLES GROOM. I am the son of Richard Groom, a gun-maker, of Lemon-street, Whitechapel. On the 3rd of March the prisoner came to the shop; my brother went out with him to take this gun.
WILLIAM THOMAS GROOM. I was in the shop; the prisoner came in, and said he wanted to purchase a gun for a gentleman; my brother looked out two - he said if I would go with him to the gentleman in Drury-lane, he would choose one, and send back the money by me; I went with him, and in Fenchurch-street he said; very likely the gentleman might he at his father's livery stables; he took me to Underwood's stables, in Duke's-place - he took one gun off my shoulder, and went in with it, saying, if the gentlemen liked it he would bring me out the money - I waited ten minutes, and then found he had gone through the stable, and out of another door in Mitre-street.
Prisoner. Q. Was not that gun fairly bought? A. No - he was a stranger to me; he came to the shop alone- he was not trusted with it, but was to bring me back the money or this gun.
JOSEPH SHERWOOD. I am a pawnbroker, and live in the London-road. On the 3rd of March, about eight o'clock in the evening, the prisoner pawned the gun.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I considered it was fairly bought- it was to be paid for by concussion caps; I returned to the gate in ten minutes, and he was gone - I had only been to Mitre-street, where my mother lives; the officer said I should not be prosecuted if I told where the gun was; and the father said he would forego proceedings if I got it back.
GUILTY. Aged 22. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300415-337]
1003. LEWIS HENRY was again indicted for stealing, on the 24th of December, 4 pairs of boots, value 3l. 3s.; 2 pairs of shoes, value 14s., and 1 bag, value 1s., the goods of Edward Cheaper.
EDWARD CHEAPER. I am a boot-maker. On the 24th of December the prisoner, whom I had seen once before, came and selected four pairs of boots, and two pairs of shoes; he directed me to send my lad with him to Langbourn-court, Fenchurch-street - I gave him an invoice of the goods; and gave my lad goods, and a receipt for the money, to deliver to the prisoner - he fully understood it was forready money, and said if I wrote a receipt, there would be a gentleman there who would pay the lad; they went away together - the lad returned with out money or boots - I apprehended the prisoner this day fortnight.
EDWARD GIBBS. I am the prosecutor's errand-boy. I was to go to Langbourn-court with the prisoner; master gave me a receipt to give the gentleman for the money - the prisoner heard that; I was not to part withthem without the money - the prisoner took me down Jewry-street, and gave me a shilling to get changed, as he wanted some halfpence, and said he would hold the bag the while; I went into a public-house - they could not give me change, and when I came out the prisoner was gone with the goods.
Prisoner. The prosecutor has known me a long time; I have been frequently at his shop.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Transported for Seven Years for each offence.
[trialAccount t18300415-338]
1004. THOMAS OVERALL was indicted for stealing, on the 3rd of March, 7 3/4 lbs. of hog's lard, value 4s., the goods of Mary Lyons.
MARY LYONS. I keep a cheesemonger's shop in Winchester-street, London-wall. On the 3rd of March, between eight and nine o'clock in the morning, I was in the parlour - an officer brought the prisoner in with this bladder of lard, and I missed one from my window.
ABRAHAM HAM. I am an officer. I saw the prisoner walk into the prosecutor's shop and take this lard from the window - he ran across the street, and I secured him with it.
The prisoner put in a written defence, stating that he had been ruined by the failure of the Wellingborough Bank. He received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 42. - Strongly recommended to mercy.
Fined One Shilling and Discharged.

1005. EDMUND LAURENCE was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of March, 1 handkerchief, value 6d., the goods of William Hall, from his person.
WILLIAM HALL. On the 20th of March, about half-past nine o'clock in the morning, I was in Cheapside, and heard the officer call, Stop sir! - I turned round and he was scuffling with the prisoner, who I saw throwing my handkerchief down; I had not felt it taken.
ROBERT TYRRELL. I am an officer. I was in Cheapside, and saw the prisoner in company with another, following close behind a gentleman - I followed him a few yards, then saw him put his left hand into the gentleman's pocket,' take his handkerchief and secrete it under his coat; I collared him, he struggled to get away, and at the same time threw the handkerchief behind him - the other ran off; I called the gentleman, who took the handkerchief up and gave it to me.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I saw a person run across the road - I had not gone two steps before this man took me, and he laid down the handkerchief himself.
GUILTY. Aged 16. - Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18300415-340]
1006. JOHN WILKIE was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of April, 1 tin box, value 1d.; 2 sovereigns, and 9s., the property of James Kalman, from his person.
JAMES KALMAN. I am a labourer. On the 7th of April, between eleven and twelve o'clock in the day, I was in the street with a tin box in my side-pocket, containing two sovereigns and 9s. - as I crossed the street from a coach, there was a crowd, and before I could get in among the crowd, I felt a hand at my pocket and missed my box; it was picked up with nothing in it but a ticket - I took the prisoner's hand out of my pocket; he ran off - I followed him, and he was taken without getting out of my sight; I did not see him throw the box down.
JOHN FORRESTER. I am an officer. On the 7th of April, I saw the prisoner running very hard and looking very agitated - the prosecutor was behind him calling Stop thief! I said, "What have you lost?" he said a tin box - I called out, Stop him! and a man stopped him: two more ran away down Old Gravel-lane, behind Houndsditch, and the box was found thrown over a yard in that direction.(Box produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. When I was taken I was quite round another street - several people were running.
GUILTY. Aged 23. - Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18300415-341]
1007. JOSEPH HARROW was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of February, 1 handkerchief, value 3s., the goods of Peter Davie, from his person.
PETER DAVIE. I am a shoemaker. On the 21st of February, about one o'clock, I was coming out of Christchurch, Newgate-street, the officer called my attention to the prisoner, and I saw my handkerchief found on him - I had not felt it taken.
JOHN LACY HAWKINS. I am a marshalsman. The Lord Mayor and Sheriffs were attending a charity sermon at Christchurch; the congregation were coming out - I heard some complaint in the church; the prisoner was in the porch, coming out - I looked over, and saw him stooping his head down, and walking softly; he had a leather cap on - I could not see his hands; his cap was moving - I could not account for it; I took hold of both his arms, and said, "How did you come to church?" he said, "Because I heard the Lord Mayor was coming;" I said, "Where do you live?" he said over the water - I took him to the lodge of Christ's-hospital, searched him, and found two handkerchiefs on him; while I was questioning him the prosecutor came up, and claimed one.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence (written.) I went to Christ's-hospital to hear Divine service; when the service was over, they came out in a great confusion - coming along the church-way out I saw a handkerchief lay under my feet; I of couse picked it up, and put it into my pocket, with a full view, after I got out of the church, to make it known to some person, for advice what to do with it, but some person who saw me pick up the said handkerchief, no doubt, gave information to the beadle.
MR. DAVIE. My handkerchief was in my right-hand pocket; it could not have dropped out.
GUILTY. Aged 17. - Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18300415-342]
1008. JOSEPH BARTLETT and MICHAEL HILL were indicted for stealing, on the 25th of March, 56 lbs. of coffee, value 2l., and 1 bag, value 6d., the goods of Richard Roach.
RICHARD ROACH. I am a grocer, and live in Fenchurch-street. I was engaged with a gentleman at the back part of my premises, and saw Hill pass several times - I then saw him stoop and take this bag of coffee from the door; it was just within the threshold - I went out, and saw Bartlett with it, in the possession of Herdsfield -Hill was running away, but was stopped, and both secured.
CHARLES HERDSFIELD. I am an officer. I saw Hill standing at Mr. Roach's window; I passed him, crossedover, and saw him carrying the coffee down the street; he put it into Bartlett's arms about two doors from the shop: I crossed and took Bartlett - I tried to take Hill, but he stooped down, and I missed him; he was stopped within a few yards.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Bartlett's Defence. I was merely passing along, and this young man asked me to give him a lift with the bag.
CHARLES HERDSFIELD. Bartlett received it into his arms, and I laid hold of him - I had not seen them speak to each other, but he was standing still about two houses off.
Hill put in a written Defence, stating that a man had employed him to carry the bag, and that he asked Bartlett to help it on his shoulder.
HILL - GUILTY. Aged 19.
Confined Six Months.
BARTLETT - NOT GUILTY.

1009. JAMES COUNTY was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of April, 1 handkerchief, value 3s., the goods of William Robertson Hayward, from his person.
WILLIAM ROBERTSON HAYWARD, ESQ. I live in the Temple. On the 15th of April, about eight o'clock in the evening, I was in Bridge-street, and felt my pocket lighten- I instantly turned round, and seized the prisoner; I saw my handkerchief in his hand - he put it behind him, dropped it, and struggled hard to get away, but I secured him, and gave him in charge.
RICHARD ANDREWS. I received the prisoner in charge.(Property produced and sworn to.)

1010. JAMES WALTER TOOMES was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of March, 1 great coat, value 5l., the goods of George Davison.
JAMES MILBANK. I am footman to Mr. George Davison. On the 12th of March I came with the carriage to Mr. Helps, Bartholomew-hospital, and then went to my master's town house, to fetch his great coat, which I put into the carriage, under the back seat; I left the carriage at the bottom of Fore-street, and went to Moorgate; the coachman came to me in five minutes, and said the coach was robbed - the coat has never been found; it was a dark olive, and rolled up like a bundle.
WILLIAM FLETCHER. I am the coachman - it was a job-coach; I was in the Castle, at Moorgate, waiting for Milbank - I came out, and saw the carriage door had been opened, and the great coat gone; I had not been away five minutes - Brown came and asked if I had lost any thing, and gave me information.
ELEANOR BROWN. I live in the neighbourhood. This carriage stood at the public-house door, and nobody with it; I knew the prisoner by his using the house at the corner of the court where I live - I saw him open the carriage door, and bring out the coat; I was not two yards from him - it was a dark coat - he walked away with it; I went on an errand, and saw the coachman in a quarter of an hour, and told him; I should have called Stop thief! but I was in a great hurry to go on an errand.
Prisoner. Her statement is untrue; at the Mansion-house she said she could not tell what it was; at eight o'clock at night it was impossible for her to swear to any body.
ELEANOR BROWN. I do not say it was a coat; I saw him take something out, rolled up like a bundle.
ABRAHAM HAM. I am an officer. I apprehended the prisoner on the 17th; Brown had given me information on the 12th - I went to look for a man named Thomas, at the Hinds public-house that night, understanding her to say it was Thomas, but it seems she said Thomes.
GUILTY. Aged 23. - Transported for Seven Years.

1011. JONATHAN THOMAS was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of March, 1 hat, value 1l., the goods of Alexander Glenday.
ALEXANDER GLENDAY. I lodge in Seacoal-lane - the prisoner slept in the same room; my hat was in the cupboard in the room. I missed it between nine and ten o'clock at night - he had come home between nine and ten, and went out again about ten - he had gone to bed, and got up again; he did not sleep in the same bed - I do not know whether he had undressed, but his bedclothes were turned down, and the bed was warm - I missed the hat directly he was gone; I went out, and found him going into a pawnbroker's shop with a white bundle, and asked if he did not lodge in Seacoal-lane; he said No (he had only been there four nights) - I asked him to come to the house, which he did; the landlord said he was the man - I then asked what he had done with my hat; he said he had pawned it in Turnmill-street, Clerkenwell.
Prisoner. He promised, if I confessed, he would not prosecute me. Witness. I said if he had pawned it, and gave me the ticket, I would let him go - he said he had torn the ticket up.
JOHN JONES, I am the landlord of the house - the prisoner described himself as a porter to Mr. Sims, of St. Paul's church-yard - I sent my wife there, and heard her tell him she could find no such person.
EDWARD BARBER. I am a shopman to Mr. Cross, of Turnmill-street. The prisoner pawned this hat for 6s., in the name of Henry Jones, Peter-street.(Property produced and sworn to.)
WILLIAM TURNBULL. I received the prisoner in charge, and found 5s. 9d. on him - he said he had torn the duplicate up.
The prisoner pleaded distress.
GUILTY. Aged 21. - Confined Two Months.

1012. HUGH HUGHES was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of April, 1 handkerchief, value 6d., the goods of George Laurence, from his person.
GEORGE LAURENCE. I am employed as a warehouseman, in Cheapside. On the 8th of April, a little after nine o'clock in the evening. I was in Aldersgate-street, and the officer accosted me - I went back with him, and he took the prisoner a few yards off - I saw him find my handkerchief in his pocket; he said nothing - I had not felt it taken, but had used it a very few minutes before.
THOMAS BICKNELL. I am an officer. On the 8th of April, about nine o'clock in the evening, I saw the prisoner in company with another lad - I observed the prisoner put his hand into Laurence's pocket, and take out the handkerchief; I followed Laurence, brought him back, and found the prisoner with his hand in anothergentleman's pocket, at the very moment, in the act of taking another handkerchief - I took his companion, but he was discharged: I found the prosecutor's handkerchief in the prisoner's pocket.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I had been out of place for six months - I have no father; I was trying for fifteen weeks to go to the Marine Society, but was refused by the doctor; I was told if I committed any act, that would send me there, or to the Refuge - I should be happy to get my living honestly.
THOMAS BICKNELL. I found his statement, about the Marine Society, true - his companion was thirteen years old.
GUILTY. Aged 17. - Judgment Respited .
[trialAccount t18300415-347]
1013. JOHN DULLAGE was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of March, 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of James William Thompson, from his person.
JAMES WILLIAM THOMPSON. I live at Poplar. On the 27th of March, about seven o'clock in the evening, I was near Aldgate church, and felt a pull at my pocket - I turned round, and saw the prisoner in possession of my handkerchief, which was safe just before; I collared him, and was immediately surrounded by five or six persons, men and boys - I asked where I could get an officer; they directed me up an alley, and I was glad to get back again- I was surrounded by them, saying I had better let him go, as I should have so much trouble; but an officer came, and I delivered him over.
Prisoner. A lady saw it drop from his pocket. Witness. I saw it drop from the prisoner's hand.
JAMES HALL. I am a constable of Tower-ward. I took the prisoner near Aldgate church; there were a great many men and women in a waggon, going off to the Swan River, which caused a crowd.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I never saw the gentleman's handkerchief; I was walking by, when he turned round and said, "You are the lad;" he instantly collared me, and his handkerchief laid behind me - a lady came to the watch-house, and said she saw it fall from his pocket.
CAROLINE BAILEY. I live at No. 20, Princes-street, Baker's-row, Whitechapel, and live with my mother - I get my living by needle-work; I never knew the prisoner before this occurred. I saw Mr. Thompson drop his handkerchief, whether he was putting it into his pocket or taking it out, I do not know; but I saw him drop it - there were a number of people round a waggon; the prisoner was coming by at the time - Mr. Thompson caught hold of him by the arm and said, "I have got you, you have robbed me;" I said, "No, sir, he has not, indeed;" he said, "No, has not he?" I said, "No, sir, the handkerchief dropped from your pocket;" he said, "Well, we will see all about that," and conveyed the prisoner to the watch-house.
WILLIAM THOMPSON. I saw this witness; and she intimated to me that it had dropped from my pocket; but I saw the boy drop it, and immediately collared him, and took him up the alley - this young woman followed, and said, "You are mistaken in the boy."
Q. Not that you was mistaken in his person, but mistaken in his being the thief? A. Yes.
CAROLINE BAILEY. I told him no such thing; no person was near his pocket at the time - I did not go before the Magistrate, as I thought the gentleman had declined it; and I knew the boy was not guilty - I told this story at the watch-house, and left word with the officer, where I was to be found.
NOT GUILTY.
